# Questions

Each one has the default used so the build could keep going. Answer here or in the spec.

Everything before batch 59 (questions 1 to about 470, Oct 28 to Nov 6 2026) is in `docs/archive/QUESTIONS-to-2026-11-06.md`. The numbering carries on from there.

## Waiting on Eli: yes or no (Nov 27 2026)

Each line is one question. Yes or no answers it. The most important is first. The number at the end is the item below with the detail.

1. Should the moves you count before FI, and the Yes, but card, move the two numbers at the top? (Yes means one calculation is chosen for both. Today they show on their own line.) (649e, 650d, 651g)
2. Are the 2026 federal tax figures approved as listed, including the two cutoffs that could not be read from a second source ($403,550 for a joint return, $256,200 for head of household)? (648i)
3. Should health insurance before 65 and tax on withdrawals stay out of the first number until the player taps "Count this"? (645a)
4. Is "spending drops to 75% of today's from age 85" approved? (No means it stops at 85% from age 75.) (646a)
5. Should state income tax be counted from the regular pay table, knowing it runs high for retirement income? (No means federal tax only for now.) (648d)
6. For New York head of household, is the reference file right ($11,200 and its own cutoffs) and the game's tax engine to be changed to match? (648e)
7. Should the flow get one more tap ("Where is your invested money?") so a new player can see a tax drop? (648b)
8. Should the in-debt version (Phase 6: a debt-free date in place of the FI number for a player with debt at 8% or more) be built next? It was skipped in this run.
9. The web app's front page (one.html) shows the LGBTQ+ money tools to everyone today, and so does its What's new. Hide them there too? (653d)
10. Do you own stresslessaboutmoney.com, and can you sign in to the place where its DNS is managed? (582a)
11. Are you on a paid Zoom plan? (592n)
12. Should the calmer bar end on a last day, such as day 30, for a player who never reaches the reveal? (652c)
13. Should "My number" stay as the fourth button of the first-week bar? (652a)
14. Should the top 3 moves be added to the plan by themselves at the reveal, with no tap? (651e)
15. Should the shared cards stay without dollar amounts? (651b)

Open and not a yes or no: whose name goes on the credit of the Yes, but idea? The file gives no candidate (650a).
Next in line, left off the 15: should the game get the part-time pay taps and the Fat list, so Barista, Chubby and Fat have a number there? (644f)

## Batch 59 (Nov 6 2026): the workbook's tabs follow the session, Google Sheets first

471. **Credit cards are rows of the Debt table.** Every debt formula reads one Debt table (a named range is one column on one tab), so the cards are its first band on Own and owe, with Your credit and what your cards give you right under the debts. **Proposed:** keep one table. Built that way.
472. **Where the leftovers went.** Calendar entries and Month end sit at the bottom of Money out; Benefits now and what a raise does on Money in; the fire drill and the lender's view at the bottom of Own and owe; the Scorecard and Current vs Proposed on The plan; Life Transitions on The future; Discovery and the Phase 4 switch at the bottom of You; the metrics, ranges and the program on the One-pager. OK, or should some of these move (or leave the sheet)? **Proposed:** keep for now; move when the app's nav is rebuilt to match.
473. **Column headers use the field names, not always the app's column words.** Sections, tab names, form rows and results use the app's words; a table's headers are the registry's labels ("Payment a month", "DRAFTT letter") where the screen says "Payment / mo" or "Spending group". The import reads by hidden keys, so renaming is safe. Rename them in a later pass? **Proposed:** yes, with the nav rebuild.
474. **Input tabs are not locked.** A protected tab will not let you delete a row that holds a formula cell (Excel), and Google Sheets turns protection into a warning anyway, so tabs with boxes stay open; the look carries the meaning (blue bordered box: yours; grey, no border: worked out), and typing over a grey cell is listed and never saved. Only Statements, the One-pager, Math and the hidden tabs are protected, with no password. OK? **Proposed:** yes.
475. **Income bands: one per person and type.** "Robin: Job", "Robin: Self-employed", "Lee: Partner job" for two adults; "Job", "Rental" for one; one blank row each, so a new row under "Side hustle" is a side hustle. Or one band per person with the type picked on the row? **Proposed:** keep person and type.
476. **Money shows as whole dollars.** $1,650 like the app; a cell with cents (typed or worked out) shows them, and the rows the app shows in cents always do. OK? **Proposed:** yes.
477. **Checked against Google Sheets by reading, not by running.** There is no Google Sheets in the build container, so the formulas are recalculated in LibreOffice and checked against Google's function list and its rules (no implicit intersection, names, dropdowns from ranges) by tools/sheets-check.mjs. Open one exported file in Google Sheets yourself (upload to Drive) and look at a Check column or two? **Proposed:** yes, once, on the Demo.
478. **The self-serve journey (start.html) keeps a few things outside the household file.** The Whole life choices (withdrawal strategy, claim age, conversions, stock share, health cost before 65), the scenario blocks, and the spending parts from "Help me add it up" are saved in this browser under `slam.start`, not in the client file, so they do not export, do not reach the workbook, and the coach app cannot see them. Moving them into the file means new registered fields and spec rows. **Proposed:** do it once the screens settle.
479. **The whole-life engine (js/lifetime) is not in the math registry or the workbook.** It is new math beside js/math (year by year to 90, withdrawal strategies, bracket filling, the history replay), with its own tests (test/lifetime.test.js), and it reads js/math's tax functions. House rule says math lives in js/math and is registered with a sheet formula. **Proposed:** register it (as fromApp, since a year loop is not a cell formula) when it has been used with a few real plans.
480. **What the whole-life engine leaves out.** Gains in a taxable account are not taxed when sold; state rules that exclude retirement income are not applied; no required minimum distributions; health cost before 65 is the person's own guess with no marketplace subsidy math; Roth money held today is treated as growth (not reachable before 59 and a half); a couple is one person. Each understates or overstates tax a little; none is hidden on the screen's notes yet except the first two in the file header. **Proposed:** add subsidy math and taxable gains next.
481. **A card's 0% offer is recorded but the payoff math still charges the full rate during it** (js/math/debt.js reads promoEnd only to put the card first when the offer is about to end). Found from the journey's level 3. **Proposed:** charge promoApr until promoEnd in the payoff plan.
482. **Market history is Shiller's data, January to January, 1871 to 2022** (data/history-returns.json, from github.com/datasets/s-and-p-500). Bond returns are worked out from the long rate, not a published index. **Proposed:** fine for a stress test; replace with a published total-return series if one is licensed.
483. **The self-serve journey's choices now live in the household file** (`settings.start`: what brought you here, what matters most, a home priced, the value marks, drills ticked, the whole-life choices, the last statement's totals), so a backup carries them and the coach app can read them. It is one registered key marked not-in-sheet. Only which plan is open, the screen you were on and whether the tour ran stay with the browser. This answers 478 for those choices; should any of them (the whole-life choices most of all) become real fields with spec rows?
484. **The home what-if (js/home.js) sits beside js/math, not in the math registry or the workbook.** Its one formula is a loan's level payment, which is `loanFromPayment` (js/math/lender.js) run the other way, and it uses the lender view's rate, term and taxes-and-insurance share (RULES). Mortgage insurance under 20% down, closing costs and HOA are named on the screen but not in the number. Register it as its own formula with a sheet column?
485. **The 25 worries' first steps are general education written without a source list.** Each needs Eli's read before a stranger relies on it, the same as the credits page.
486. **The self-serve screens are not in `tools/e2e/layout.cjs`.** They were walked at 390, 1280 and 1440 by a separate script (no sideways scroll, every tap target 44px or more, no text under 14px); the QA.md 12-category score for them is still to do.
487. **The home what-if's starting guesses have no source yet.** Mortgage insurance 0.5% of the loan a year, closing costs 3% of the price, homes up 3% a year, investments up 7% a year, rent up 3% a year. Each is a tap to change and is named on the screen, but the house rule wants each in RULES with a source and date. Which sources do you want (Freddie Mac, Urban Institute for mortgage insurance, Case-Shiller for home growth)?
488. **Own or rent leaves out selling costs and the tax side** (the mortgage interest deduction, the gain exclusion on a home sale). It says so on screen.
489. **A statement read on the self-serve side keeps only the totals by category** (the top 12), not the transactions, and it replaces the spending number from memory when you tap Use. The coach app's importer keeps every row. Should the journey hand the file to that importer instead, so both sides hold the same rows?
490. **Zero pay with "I have a job" picked cannot be saved as an answer**; the screen now points to "I am between jobs". Is there a third case to add (no pay and not looking: a student, a stay-at-home parent)?
491. **Save across devices is a file for now** (Download a backup, Load a backup). A private link would need either the plan packed into the link itself or a server; which do you want?
492. **Answered Nov 12 (Eli): the home guesses stand, and each is editable.** They are now in RULES (homePmi 0.5%, homeClosing 3%, homeGrowth 3%, homeInvestGrowth 7%, homeRentGrowth 3%) with rows on the assumptions list, and every one is a tap on the home card, rent growth included. Answers 487. Still open: a published source for each.
493. **Answered Nov 12 (Eli): no pay is its own answer.** The income question has a one-tap "No pay right now" (a student, a parent at home), so nobody is stuck or pushed into "between jobs". Answers 490.
494. **Answered Nov 12 (Eli): a private link.** "Copy a private link" packs the plan (without its undo history) into the link itself, about 1,200 characters for a new plan. Nothing is uploaded; whoever has the link has the plan; opening it asks first and then takes the plan out of the address bar. Answers 491. A short link would need a server.
495. **The layout check now walks the self-serve screens** (`Start` in tools/e2e/layout.cjs: 11 screens, 3 widths, light and dark, folds closed and open). Answers 486. One check still flags the home card: the pressed chip sits in a different column on each row, which is what a row of choices does. Leave it listed, or teach the check that chip rows are choices, not a table?
496. **"Talk it through with Eli" on the one-page summary opens stresslessaboutmoney.com.** Is that the right place, or should it be a booking link?
497. **Answered Nov 12 (Eli): the Lifetime plan's choices are the household's own.** The withdrawal strategy, starting rate, claim age, conversions, stock share, health cover, spending drift and stop age are saved in the household's assumptions (lifeStrategy and the rest), marked app-only, so the coach app reads them. The layered life events stay with the journey's choices for now. Answers 483.
498. **Answered Nov 12 (Eli): the lifetime engine is registered.** It moved to js/math (lifetime.js, stress.js) as M9U and M9V, app-only with the reason, with rows on the spec's Math tab and export format 52. Answers 479.
499. **Answered Nov 12 (Eli): 0% offers.** The payoff plan charges the promo rate (0 when blank) until the offer ends, then the card's own rate. Answers 481. The workbook's M34 formula still charges the full rate from month one; mirror it there?
500. **The screen is now called Lifetime**, because "whole life" reads as whole life insurance. There is a new worry, "I was sold whole life insurance", with why many people leave it and six steps out. Its wording needs Eli's read (the read-through spreadsheet).
501. **Home guesses now carry sources** (Freddie Mac for mortgage insurance, FHFA for home prices, BLS for rent, IRS Topics 701 and 409 for the sale). One changed: homes go up 4% a year, not 3%, because FHFA's record since 1991 is 4.3% to 4.4%. Investments stay at 7% as Eli's guess. Own or rent now takes selling costs (6%) and tax on a gain past the tax-free amount, and shows year one's interest next to the standard deduction. Answers 487 and 488.
502. **A statement read on the journey now keeps every row** in the household file through the coach app's importer (deduped), not matched to budget lines. Answers 489. Should the journey also match them to lines?
503. **Left as it is for now (Eli, Nov 12):** the layout check's flag on the home card's chip rows (495), and "Talk it through with Eli" opening stresslessaboutmoney.com (496).
504. **The lifetime engine's gaps are filled (Eli, Nov 12).** Gains on taxable money are taxed when sold (0%, 15%, 20% bands, stacked on ordinary income; what you have today counts as already taxed); required withdrawals start at 73, or 75 for someone born 1960 or later, and what is not needed moves to taxable; the 2026 Marketplace credit lowers cover before 65 (off past four times the poverty line); a second person adds their benefit, joint taxes, cover until 65 and their years. Roth money put in was already free to take out. Answers 480. The 2026 bands, the withdrawal table, the poverty guideline and the credit table sit in js/math/lifetime.js with their sources, not in data/reference-2026.json: move them there?
505. **Still left out of the lifetime plan:** the years after one of two people dies (one benefit, single brackets), yearly dividends taxed along the way, the 3.8% investment income tax, Medicare's income surcharge, Medicaid under the poverty line, state rules that skip retirement income, and inflation's share of a gain (the plan is in today's dollars, so taxed gains read a little low). Which matter to you?
506. **Six new tools on the journey (Eli, Nov 12), in js/extras.js beside js/math.** The spend-more number (the most a month you could add and still have work optional by an age; it runs the journey's own what-if), what fees cost (the same money grown with and without a yearly fee, against a 0.05% index fund), which move fits my life (six moves sized from the household; their effects on time, calm, people, health, freedom and cushion are the usual ones from the SPARKS tradeoff notes, not measured), the checkup (11 things a planner asks), what actually happens (a missed card payment, a collector's call, a tax bill: written from the CARD Act, the FDCPA and IRS penalty rules, without links yet), and a money meeting for two (eight taps each, carried in a link). All of the wording needs Eli's read. Register the first two as formulas?
507. **Seven more tools (Eli, Nov 12), also in js/extras.js.** Which account gets my next dollar (five taps; the Roth limit is named as about $153,000 on your own and $242,000 together for 2026), the climb (above zero, $10,000, $100,000, a million, at what is kept each month before growth), what a raise is worth (counts 30% to taxes, a guess, and all the rest invested; with four lines on how to ask), if I start now, my parents and my plan, the monthly money date (calm and joy from 1 to 5, kept for 24 months in the household file), and a spending-is-up line when spending rose 5% or more since the last visit. Wording and the 30% guess need Eli's read.
508. **Four more pieces (Eli, Nov 12).** Money over time on See (the plan's own order, M79, as phases with dates: a road, buckets, or a list). A picture switcher: the same number can be shown several ways and the choice is remembered (so far the percent-of-the-way picture as ring, bar, thermometer or bucket, and money over time). Design my life (a life at an age, priced, and what it asks each month from now; in today's dollars, without taxes or Social Security, which the Lifetime plan has). Compare two choices (pay, spending, and three day-to-day marks for each, the date for each, and a saved receipt of the one chosen). Which other pictures should get the switcher first?
509. **Line by line, and three more cards (Eli, Nov 12).** See has a chart of every part of the money by year (invested, what you put in, growth, cash, debt, net worth), each line a tap on or off, also as numbers or in words. It is a simple walk beside the engine (js/extras.js seriesOf), not the engine's own month-by-month path: should it read the engine's path instead? What if also has Can I afford this (one purchase against cash and what is kept), Subscriptions that do the same job, and My pay is uneven (pay yourself halfway between a slow and a usual month, hold two months as a buffer: a rule of thumb that needs Eli's call).
510. **What if is sorted (Eli, Nov 12).** Its 13 tools sit in four folded groups (Spending, Earning, Big choices, People) under a "For you" section: the three that fit what brought the person here, or what matters most to them when no worry is picked (js/worries.js WORRY_TOOLS, MIRROR_TOOLS). More is grouped the same way (You, Plan ahead, Keep it going, About). Are the three picked for each of the 26 worries the right three?
511. **With no pay on file the engine's savings rate is not a number** (M-something in js/math/metrics.js divides by take-home pay of zero). The self-serve screens now show "needs an answer" there. Should the engine itself return no value in that case, and what does the workbook show?
512. **You are here (Eli, Nov 12).** The plan now opens with one card: where the person is, one thing to do next (with how long it takes), and a fold of what they have done. The order is: birth year, the five questions, the first step on what brought them here, a statement, each level in turn, the checkup, then this month's money date. Is that the right order, and should the card also sit on the other screens?
513. **Next-step strip and "solid for now" (Eli, Nov 12).** Every screen after the first two carries a slim Next strip (one button, plus Hide); Hide turns it off everywhere and the plan and More offer "Show my next step". On What if, and on the statement and checkup cards in Sharpen, any card can be marked "solid for now": it goes grey and quiet with a Change button, and the screen counts how many are solid. The mark remembers pay, spending, cash, investments and debt; when any of them moves, the card wakes up and asks for another look. Practice counts drills and skills done. Should levels, the five questions and the Lifetime choices get the same mark?
514. **Your map (Eli, Nov 13).** A new screen shows the whole journey as a web in six tiers (what you tell it, your plan, see it, make it sharp, decide, the long view): 26 points, each done, ready, or waiting on an answer, with lines from what feeds a point to what it feeds. Tapping a point shows what goes into it, what it still needs, what it leads to, and a button that goes there (or to the missing answer). The web is data in js/map.js. Is this the right set of points, and should the map become the home screen?
515. **The next step pulses, and the steps are counted (Eli, Nov 13).** One next step is worked out once (js/extras.js guideOf) and it pulses wherever it shows: the button on the plan's card, the Next strip, a dot on the tab that leads to it, its point on the map, and the thing to tap once you arrive. The progress line reads "Step 8 of 18" and ticks up as each one is finished. Hide turns all of it off. Motion is replaced by a still outline for people who ask their device for less motion. Is one pulse at a time right, or should each screen also pulse its own next thing?
516. **One pulse, always, on every screen (Eli, Nov 13).** After a tap the next thing is already pulsing: the first screen pulses the birth decade, then the year, then the button; a question pulses its box, then Save once there is a number; an open level pulses the next unanswered field, then Save. When the next step lives on the screen you are on, the thing itself pulses and the Next strip stays still. One pulse at a time, never two.
517. **What you owe is a list by kind (Eli, Nov 13).** Question 5 and level 3 now show Credit cards, Student loans, Car loans, Home loans and Other debts, each with its own Add button. A row asks what you owe first; level 3 adds the rate and the payment; "More about this one" holds the rest the coach app keeps (a 0% offer, limit, minimum, due day, autopay, yearly fee; federal or private, plan, PSLF, when first borrowed, paused, years left). Every field is one the coach app already has, so nothing new was registered. A car loan or mortgage added here makes the car or home too (and marks "I own" for a mortgage), the way level 7 does. Open: should the bank names on a card (Chase, Amex and so on) come from the card library so rewards carry over?
518. **Coming back opens on the next step (Eli, Nov 13).** A returning person lands on the screen where their next step lives, with it pulsing, in place of the last screen they were on. With the guide hidden it opens where they left off, as before.
519. **Line by line: the same sections for everything (Eli, Nov 13).** A new screen (More, You, Line by line) lists what comes in (jobs, side work, other), what goes out (home, food, getting around, bills, fun, everything else), what you have (checking and savings, plans at work, IRAs, brokerage, HSA) and what you owe, each kind with its own Add button, each row slim until tapped, the coach app's deeper fields behind "More about this one". Level 5 uses the investing sections. Lines for spending sit beside the rough number until the person taps "Use my lines" (the coach app's own switch). A total typed earlier for investments shrinks as accounts are listed, so nothing counts twice. Things you own (homes, cars, other things) and insurance (health; life and disability; home, car and umbrella) are lists too. Insurance takes a yes or a no as an answer and asks the cost only on a yes; its premiums follow the coach app's rules (they join the lines only when the plan runs on lines).
520. **How sure, and the whole picture (Eli, Nov 13: "the different levels of things").** Each line can be marked a guess, roughly, or known (the coach app's Gut, Rough, Know it), and the coach app's own Whole picture measure shows on Line by line and Sharpen: how much is in, how much of that is solid, a five-part bar, and the three things that would sharpen it most, each one a tap to the line it is about. The twelve coaching sessions are not shown in the self-serve app: should a person on their own see "session 1 of 12" style chapters, or is the step counter enough?
521. **Krug walks, five strangers at phone size (Nov 13).** What tripped them and what changed: (1) Alexis, card debt, avoids looking: the plan opened on a chore ("use a statement") before her answer, and the answer was "work optional at 83". Now the plan opens with the slim Next strip and the plan itself, and someone with debt and a far-off date sees the month the debt is gone first. (2) The next step said "Use a statement, so it runs on the real one"; it now says "Firm up your spending number" and points to level 2 or the lines when there is no export. (3) The active tab went dark under a finger (a hover style won); fixed. (4) Tom, the CPA: "22.0%" and "100.0%" read as noise; whole percents now show whole. (5) A sentence on Sharpen still said the statement upload was "the next thing being built"; fixed. (6) What if: the "solid for now" count wrapped into a one-word column; fixed. (7) Practice showed its count twice; fixed. Not changed, for Eli: the fuller "You are here" card now sits under the first plan card (the strip on top carries the pulse). Say if you want it back on top.
522. **Two new tools in What if (Nov 13).** "When can I stop?" lists every finish line on the path, soonest first, in plain words (ease off, stop saving for later, stop on needs only, stop for good, stop with room to spare), from the plan's own rungs. "What should I do before the year ends?" lists the moves with a deadline: 401(k) by the last paycheck, HSA and IRA by April 15, FSA, giving and selling at a loss by Dec 31, with the 2026 limits on file and what $1,000 before tax keeps, at the income tax rate only (Social Security and Medicare still apply to 401(k) money). The April 15 dates and the FSA and giving rules are written as words in js/extras.js, not as registered rules: move them to RULES with sources? Eli to read the wording; a traditional IRA is described as "may lower this year's tax, but with a plan at work that break fades out as income rises".
523. **Five more "what actually happens" guides, for Eli to read before they are trusted (Nov 13).** Added to Practice: my job ends; a medical bill I cannot pay; my checking account goes below zero; I miss a federal student loan payment; someone uses my card or my name. Each is written from the federal rules as I know them (unemployment timing, COBRA's 60 days and 18 months, the 60-day Marketplace window, 401(k) loan offset; hospital financial assistance and the credit bureaus' medical debt rules; the debit overdraft opt-in rule and ChexSystems; federal loan delinquency at 90 days and default at 270, 15% wage garnishment, rehabilitation; the $50 card limit and the 2-day and 60-day debit windows). None carries a source line yet, and state rules differ. Please read each and mark what to change, as with the first three.
524. **The rest of the list, done (Eli, Nov 13: "can you do it all").** Solid marks: an answered question says so and asks to be changed only if something changed; a finished level goes quiet (dashed, grey) until opened; Lifetime's three working cards can be marked solid for now like the What if cards. Picture switcher: Growth, Where it goes, Your path, Debt and the Lifetime chart each switch between the chart and a list or numbers. Map: a point that stands for several things (Levels 2 to 10, Spending choices, Earning choices, Fees and accounts) shows "3 of 9 done" and opens into its parts, each with its own mark and tap. The points were not split into separate dots: about fifty dots do not fit a phone. Say if you want them split anyway.
525. **Two more tools in What if (Nov 13).** "What will April look like?" takes one tap (federal tax held back so far, from the pay stub) and shows about how much comes back or is due, what to hold back from the last paychecks to close a gap, and whether 90% of the tax is being held back (IRS Publication 505). Federal income tax only, standard deduction, no credits; it says so. "What if I run two homes?" prices a second home for 6 months, a year, 2 years or from now on: what the left-over money covers, what comes from cash and how long cash lasts, or how far the date moves. It stands in for the "two-household plan": is that what you meant, or did you mean two people each with their own home and money planning together?
526. **Audit, Nov 13.** Five different people (typical with debt, spending more than pay at 22, 64 with $1.9M, between jobs, age only) through all 17 screens at 390, 1280 and 1440, light and dark, every chip tapped on every screen for two of them. Found and fixed: Lifetime crashed for someone between jobs when no stopping age held in history; "Of that, invested" could be larger than "Left over" (it is the amount once debt and cushion are done, and now says so); "Get the full match: Done" showed for people never asked about a match (now "Check it in level 4"); verdict words ("bad stretch", "should", "went wrong") in Lifetime, credits and two titles; the April card multiplied yearly pay by twelve before it shipped. Nothing found: sideways scroll, empty cards, unlabeled inputs, small tap targets, broken numbers. Not audited by hand: the coach app beyond its layout check.
527. **Rows as a sheet (Eli, Nov 14: "there's gotta be a better way than this").** One open card took a whole phone screen of chips. Each row is now one line of small boxes (name, then what you owe, rate, pay a month), like a spreadsheet, so five cards fit one screen. The amounts to tap show in one strip under the box being filled. The bank is a dropdown on the name. "How sure", the 0% offer and the rest sit behind More, with Take it off. A "Remove the N empty ones" button clears rows left blank. The same row is used on every list. Also: "Debt free went from X to Y to Z" now reads "was X. Now it is Y to Z."
528. **Standalone pages, carried by a link (Eli, Nov 14).** The first one is live: debt.html, "When am I debt free?" One thing: list what you owe, get the month it is gone, which one to pay first, and what extra does. The answers live in the page's own address (refresh, bookmark or send it), and "Next" carries them into the whole plan, which opens them without a question when the device has nothing yet. The pattern for the rest: js/solo.js (the order of the pages and each page's tested math), js/solo/shell.js (opens, saves to the address, hands on), one small file per page. The payoff is the engine's own payoffPlan, so a standalone and the full plan cannot disagree. A blank rate takes the typical rate for its kind and a blank payment takes the card minimum (or a ten-year payment on a loan), and the page names both. Which standalone comes second: one fire drill, "When can I stop?", or the couples money meeting?
529. **Accounts, shared accounts for couples, and the money personality quiz (Eli, Nov 14).** Not built: real accounts need a server (sign-in and a database), and the site is static pages today. Needs Eli's choice of service and his own keys. Also need the quiz itself: which one, and where it lives.
530. **A site of modules you can tap (Eli, Nov 14).** one.html is the front door: six modules in order, each one thing done right, each carrying its answers to the next in a link. (1) What is my first move: Eli's quantum collapse, five taps or fewer, each answer path-changing, a meter of plans still possible (honest arithmetic: 1,920 down to 1), early exits when being behind or between jobs already settles it, and it never tells someone on a forgiveness track to rush their loans. (2) Start at the finish line: Eli's "begin with the end in mind". What you need (a month, a year or a total), by when, what you have, then what comes in and goes out; it works backwards to the monthly amount, the gap, and three ways to close it. Same growth and withdrawal rates as the whole plan. (3) How do I handle money: the five-element quick ten (Water, Earth, Air, Fire, Spirit), scales and not types, sets what the plan shows first, with a link for a second person and a side-by-side. THE TEN STATEMENTS ARE MY DRAFT: Eli's own question bank (10, 25 and 100 items, 20 traits) lives in the earlier quiz file and should replace them. (4) When am I debt free. (5) How long would my money last. (6) The whole plan.
531. **Accounts, as far as they can go without a server (Eli, Nov 14).** "Make my account" on the front door keeps your place on this device: every module opens where you left off, the front door shows what is done, and it counts the answers you did not have to type twice (about 30 seconds each, a plain estimate). A shared space ("Ours") holds one plan for two; until sign-in exists it travels by link. The model (js/accounts.js: mine is mine, ours is open to both members and nobody else) sits behind an adapter, and the server adapter (js/cloud.js) and the database rules (docs/cloud-schema.sql) are written for Supabase but have never run against a live project. To turn it on: create the project, run the SQL, put the address and public key in data/cloud.json, then the sign-in screen gets built and tested (about 5 units). No name is asked for yet: the account is called "Me".
532. **Panel audit of the modules (Nov 14).** Money Guy: my first-move order had card debt ahead of the match; it now follows the plan's own order (a first cushion, the full match, then expensive debt). Tom: the finish line now says tax on what you take out later is not counted. Krug: on the front door the modules come first and the account pitch after, until there is an account. Alexis: someone who is behind gets "steady the month first" and goes to the runway page, with no numbers asked before that. Hormozi and Gary Vee, not done: there is no "talk to Eli" button on the front door (needs the booking link) and no shareable result image for the quiz. Jonathan and Brad, not done: the 625-skill FI Skill Tree is not connected; each module could unlock its branch. Mindy: the debt page counts a mortgage as debt if you add one; should "debt free" mean everything but the mortgage by default?
533. **Found and wired in: the real quiz, the couples pass, and the 625 (Eli, Nov 14).** (a) The money quiz now runs on Eli's own Money Chart bank from the August chat: 100 statements, 25 traits, five elements, with the 10 inside the 25 inside the 100, about half reverse-keyed, scored his way (reverse the minus items, average a trait, average the traits into the element). It shows Sun (strongest trait), Moon (strongest Water trait) and Rising (what others see) from the 25 up. Going deeper never re-asks. Trait names were put in plain words (Tightwad/Spendthrift is "Holding on", Saving/Buffer is "Cushion", Present bias is "Living for now", Security motivation is "Safety first"). The August chat mentions later rounds of rewrites (behavioral, feeling and motivation items) that live only in the HTML file from that chat, which I cannot open: if that file exists, send it and its wording replaces this. (b) For two: after your own ten you can guess how your person would answer, and the link you send carries both. Their page shows each muscle for both of you (covered by one of you, a shared habit, or one to build together) and how well you read them. The August build also had a same-device flow, a radar picture and a share image: not rebuilt. (c) The 625: data/skill-tree.json is the 25 curriculum trees of 25 levels from the Personalfinance repository (v6.3.1), trimmed to name, what it is and the check that levels it. The new module asks up to five milestone checks a tree (levels 5, 10, 15, 20, 25), stops at the first "not yet", and gives a character level out of 625 plus one next unlock (the main path first until level 10, then the lowest tree you have not set aside). Levels land on fives; the steps between are not asked. The tiers, stories, links, avatars and belts from the full tree are not brought over.
534. **Decided by Eli, Nov 14.** "Debt free" leaves the mortgage out by default: the debt page shows the date for everything else, names the mortgage, and has a "Count it too" tap. Accounts go with the free option (Supabase's free plan).
535. **Sign-in is built and waiting for keys (Eli, Nov 14: "prep it so when I do it's all set").** The front door has the sign-in box (email, then the link or the six-digit code), background sync on every module, a second device picking up the plan, and "Copy an invite for my person" on a shared plan. All of it is tested against a stand-in server that enforces the same rules as docs/cloud-schema.sql: another person cannot read or write your space, both members of a shared one can, a third person cannot, a wrong code is refused, and a session that has run out renews itself. It has NOT run against a real Supabase project: that happens when the two values land in data/cloud.json (steps in docs/CLOUD-SETUP.md). Until then the sign-in box does not show. Sync is newest-save-wins for a whole plan: two people typing in the same minute see the later save. A plan made on a device before signing in is kept beside the account's as "From this device".
536. **Poly friendly, testable now, and a quiz to share (Eli, Nov 14).** (a) A shared plan takes any number of people and you can have several ("Ours", "Household", "Us three", "Home", "Trip fund"); one invite link works for everyone it is sent to. The quiz compares any number of people, each numbered on the bars, and the link passes along everyone so far. (b) Practice mode: one.html?practice turns on sign-in, sync and shared plans inside one browser, nothing leaves the device, the code is always 123456. Walked at 390 and 1280: three people join one plan through the same invite, a fourth who was not invited does not get in. Found and fixed on the walk: an invite opened while the page was already open did nothing (only the address changed). Limit: practice mode is one browser, so it shows the flow, not two phones; that waits on the Supabase values. (c) "Share with friends" on the quiz result opens the phone's share sheet (or copies a line for a group chat). The link carries your five scores so friends see their chart beside yours; plan numbers do not travel. Still open: a share image.
537. **For coaches and planners, first slice (Eli, Nov 14: "what would prevent this from being number 1 for CFPs, wealth firms, coaches... fix it and add next level features").** Built: pro.html, linked from the front page as "I work with clients". (a) Your card: name, practice name, booking link (https only), shown on each client plan as "You are working with" plus a "Book a time" button, and on the printed page. This also covers the open "talk to Eli" button. (b) Client plans: one shared plan per client, one invite each, a list showing where each client is, the next step, and a "Changed" tag when the client has filled something in since the coach last looked. (c) One page: a printable summary per client (first move, debt free date, months of runway, finish line), every line from the same math as the client's pages, open items named. Walked in practice mode at 390, 1280, 1440: coach signs in, adds two clients, client joins by invite and sees the coach and the booking button, coach prints the page. Layout check covers the three states. QA (the 12 categories) on pro.html: no blockers; minor: the client name box takes typing by design (a coach types a name once); Remove asks for a second tap. Not built, and why, in the reply to Eli: account linking, compliance archive, custodian and CRM links, firm seats, deep tax views in this surface. Questions: (1) Is the first buyer other coaches, or CFP firms? The roadmap differs. (2) Should the coach see everything in a client plan, or should the client choose what is shown? Today it is everything. (3) Would you pay for bank linking (Plaid has a per-account monthly cost), or stay upload and tap?
538. **Tax depth and "what moved" (Eli, Nov 14: "go for it").** Defaults taken since the three questions were not answered: built for Eli's clients first, the client and the coach both see it, tax first then "what moved". (a) Lifetime screen, "Taxes in the free years": this year's federal bracket and the dollars of room before the next one; five conversion choices side by side (none, fill 0%, 12%, and new 22% and 24%), each with everything paid over a life (income tax, the extra Medicare premium, early penalties), the age it is ahead of "no conversions", and what is left at the last year with how much of it is still to be taxed; "Dates to know" (first penalty-free Roth money, the Marketplace credit line, Medicare at 65, Social Security, the first year of an extra Medicare premium, required withdrawals). (b) New in the engine: the Medicare extra premium (Part B and D, per person, on income two years earlier, the whole step once over a line) is now taken out of the money in the free years. Table and source in the reference data and assumptions (CMS 2026; checked against several published tables that agree, not yet read off the CMS notice itself: Tom, please check). Working-year income is not counted toward it, since stopping work is a reason Social Security accepts for using the lower income; that is a simplification for someone who stops at 64 to 66 with high pay. (c) "What moved": on the coach's one page, "Mark today's session" keeps the day's numbers in the plan; the page then shows what moved since the start and since the last session (debt, the debt free month as a calendar month, cash on hand, months covered, steps done). The client sees "Since you started" on their front page. (d) Checks: by-hand tests for each rule (1,118 pass); walked a 32-year-old and a 64-year-old with $1.9M at 390, 1280, 1440 with the tax section open, no sideways scroll; layout check on the standalone pages and the lifetime screen passes (light theme only this run; the tax section is folded during that scan, so its open state rests on the walk). QA on the two screens: no blockers; the tax section holds five choices, which is long on a phone but folded by default. Panel: Tom: balances "left at 90" are not like for like (Roth against taxed-later), so each shows the part still to be taxed and a sentence says why; Money Guy: for a typical 32-year-old only the 0% fill comes out ahead, which is what the screen shows; Alexis: all of this stays folded until asked for. Not built: reading a tax return PDF (about 4 units), the next-dollar rate including lost credits (the benefits cliff tool has part of it), state-specific retirement income rules, a printable tax page for the client's CPA. Questions: (1) Should the coach's one page include the tax comparison? (2) Mark a session by a tap (today) or on every coach visit by itself?
539. **Coach's one page: tax included, session asked for (Eli, Nov 14: "Build it. Automate like a confirmation. Inc tax").** Read as: put the tax comparison on the one page, and have the page ask about marking a session instead of waiting for a tap. Built: (a) the one page now has a Taxes block: this year's bracket and room, the five conversion choices in one table (paid over a life, ahead from which age, left at the last year and how much of it is still to be taxed), the choice in the plan and the least paid one named, dates to know, and a line for the person who files the return saying what the math does and does not cover. Same numbers as the Lifetime screen (tested equal). (b) Opening a client's page asks "Is today a session with X?" with "Yes, mark today" and "Not a session"; it asks once a day per client and not for an empty plan. (c) Fixed on the walk: "no debt" now reads as an answered line, percents read 24% not 24.0%, plan totals round to the hundred. Walked with two seeded clients (64 with $1.9M; 32 with a card paid down since an August session) at 390, 1280, 1440: client front page shows "Since you started: Debt is down $1,300, Debt free 41 months sooner". Layout check on the standalone pages passes; its scan of the one page is of an empty client, the full page rests on the walk. Still not built: reading a tax return PDF (needs a PDF reader library added to a site that has none today; about 4 units), state retirement rules. Question: if "Build it" meant the PDF reader too, say so.
540. **Welcome offers and the card plan, data and math only (Eli, Nov 14: "expand the data, commit everything").** Built: data/library/card-offers.json (43 Chase cards: the 17 in the card library plus 26 it does not hold yet, each with offer, spending needed, months, fee, sources and any conflict between sites), the 5/24 slot rule, and js/math/cardplan.js with tests (what an offer is worth, fits / maybe / later for a person, the next two moves, a fee as FI number, an offer left invested). No screen reads it yet. Open: (a) the card library left bonuses out on purpose; this adds them as a separate dated layer, so say if they should stay out of client screens. (b) The floor value for each kind of point (United 1 cent, Southwest 1.2, Marriott 0.6, IHG 0.5, Hyatt 1.5, Avios and Aeroplan 1) is my assumption, to check. (c) Every offer is from review sites, none confirmed on an application page; Boundless and World of Hyatt have no value counted because the sites disagree. (d) Where the screen goes: its own standalone module ("Is this card right for me?") or inside Net worth › Credit cards. (e) The 26 cards not in the library still need their rates, credits and benefits read from Chase's pages through tools/library/cards-source.mjs.
541. **Read my tax return (Eli, Nov 14: "Yes").** Built: return.html, linked from the front page as "Read my tax return". Pick the PDF of a Form 1040; it is read in the browser (pdf.js 4.10.38, Apache-2.0, copied into vendor/pdfjs, 1.7 MB, loaded only on this page) and is not uploaded. The reader finds 16 lines by the words printed on the form (wages, interest, dividends, IRA, pensions, Social Security, gains, total income, adjusted gross income, deduction, taxable income, tax, total tax, withheld, refund, owed) and takes the amount at the right edge; typed-in form fields count too. It then says: the share of income that went to federal income tax, the bracket of the last dollars and the room before the next, a refund or balance as money a month, and taxed-later money that came out. Filing status is three chips (the reader does not pick it off the form). "Keep these in my plan" stores only the totals and the filing status; the coach's one page shows them under Taxes. Checks: 5 by-hand tests on rows laid out like the form; a walk at 390, 1280, 1440 with a made-up PDF that has a real text layer (12 of 12 filled lines read, a wrong file handled, the only request leaving the device is the font stylesheet every page loads); layout check passes. Limits, plainly: (1) not yet tried on a real return, since the IRS site is not reachable from the build machine and real returns are client data: Eli, please try your own and tell me which lines come back wrong; (2) a scan or photo has no text to read; (3) an amount under $100 with no cents reads as blank; (4) the bracket is measured against 2026 brackets whatever the return's year; (5) the totals do not yet replace the plan's own pay and tax estimate on the Lifetime screen, they sit beside it. QA on return.html: no blockers. Questions: (a) should the return's wages and filing status overwrite the plan's take-home answer when they differ, or only be shown? (b) state returns next, or W-2 and pay stub reading?
542. **W-2, pay stub and New York return readers, and gaps pointed out (Eli, Nov 14: "Do em all. Have it be shown and clearly highlighted pointed out").** The page is now "Read my papers" (return.html): pick one or several PDFs and it works out which paper each is. (a) W-2: boxes 1 to 6, 16, 17, 19 and box 12 codes D, E, AA, BB, W, DD; an amount is read right after its label or just under it in the same column. (b) Pay stub: gross, net, federal, Social Security, Medicare, state, retirement plan, health, HSA, each found by its words, this period and the year so far; "How often you get paid" is four chips and turns one pay into money a month. (c) New York IT-201: New York income, taxable income, state tax, city resident tax, total, withheld, refund, owed. (d) Gaps: an amber "Check this" card on the papers page, the front page and the coach's one page wherever a paper and the plan differ: take-home (over 5% apart), yearly wages against the plan's estimate (over 10%), this stub over a year against last year's wages (over 10%), W-2 against the return (over 1%). Nothing is overwritten on its own. The take-home gap has one button, "Use $X a month in my plan"; once a stub and the plan agree, last year's wages stop being flagged. Checks: 6 by-hand tests on pieces laid out like each paper, plus the gap rules (1,137 pass); a walk with four made-up PDFs with real text layers read in one pick at 390, 1280, 1440, all filled lines right, two gaps shown, one tap settles them, front page shows the card; layout check passes. Limits: (1) none of the four readers has seen a real paper yet (made-up layouts only; real ones are client data and the IRS and NY sites are not reachable from here), and pay stubs differ most between payroll companies, so expect misses on ADP, Paychex, Gusto and so on until real ones are tried; (2) the IT-201 wording is from memory of the form and needs checking against a real one; other states are not read; (3) scans and photos have no text to read; (4) on a W-2, an amount without cents is not read. QA on return.html: no blockers. Questions: (a) which payroll company do most of your clients have, so that stub layout is tuned first? (b) which state after New York: New Jersey?
543. **New Jersey and Connecticut returns; pay stubs kept to the lines that matter (Eli, Nov 14: "Yes new jersey and connecticut. No particular company, make sure to have just the relevant deets").** (a) The state reader now takes New York (IT-201), New Jersey (NJ-1040) and Connecticut (CT-1040): state income, taxable income, state tax, total, withheld, refund, owed (and New York's city tax). A state form is recognized before the federal one, since "NJ-1040" and "CT-1040" hold "1040" too; the federal income line printed on the Connecticut form is not taken as Connecticut income. One state return is kept at a time; a New York return kept under the earlier name still shows. (b) Pay stub: no company-specific layout. It reads only the nine lines a plan uses (gross, net, federal, Social Security, Medicare, state, retirement plan, health, HSA or FSA) and now knows more of the names payroll companies print for them (Total Gross, Direct Deposit, Fed Withholding, OASDI, 403(b), "CT State Withholding"). Checks: by-hand tests for each state and a second stub layout (1,140 pass); walked the three states at 390, 1280, 1440, kept and reloaded; layout check passes. Limits unchanged and still the main one: every reader has only seen made-up layouts. The New Jersey and Connecticut line wording is from memory of the forms and needs a real return to confirm. Question: none new; the ask stands for Eli to try real papers.
544. **"Is this card right for me?" (cards.html), the planning side of cards (Eli, Nov 14: "everything with planning, and the best resources as a supplement"; "yes do it all").** Built on the 67-card library plus the offers file: your next move in one line, the moves two steps ahead (what each opens and uses, Fits / Maybe / Later with reasons, the fee as an addition to the FI number, the offer left invested 30 years), About you taps (heavy debt, score, card spending, business, what a Chase point is worth, programs you would use, the months cards were opened for exact 5/24), Cards you hold with a yearly did-it-earn-its-fee check and a way out, Dates to know, Does any setup beat one plain card (the best 1, 2 and 3 cards from the whole library), and Tools that pair with this (data/library/card-tools.json: seven apps, no paid links, no ranking). Linked from the front page under More to explore. Calls made: airline and hotel cards stay out of the moves until their program is tapped, and one card per program in a path; credits count only when tapped; 30 years is the fixed span for "left invested". QA scorecard (cards.html): Works: pass in a browser run at 1440, 1280 and 390, light and dark, no errors. Right: math tested (test/cardplan.test.js, 11 tests); offers are from review sites, so Minor until confirmed on application pages. One answer: pass. Looks right: one phone-width look, no issues seen; not reviewed by eye at 1440 or 1280. Obvious and Easy: not think-aloud tested yet. Findable: Minor, one link on the front page and none yet from Net worth > Credit cards. Plain words: pass by read-through. Tells you: pass. Fits everywhere: no sideways scroll, nothing past the edge, no clipped buttons in my own check; tools/e2e/layout.cjs did not finish in this workspace (the browser context closed on it) and its walk does not include the standalone pages, so it is not a pass. Fast: the plain-card check is cached; not timed. Coachable: not tried in a session. Open: (a) the answers on this page stay in this browser only; they are not in the household plan or the link, unlike the other standalone pages. Should they be? (b) Spending for the plain-card check is tapped, not read from the Budget yet. (c) A link from Net worth > Credit cards. (d) Welcome offers exist for Chase only; the other 11 banks show in Cards you hold and the plain-card check but have no moves yet. (e) The 26 Chase cards not in the library have offers but no credits or benefits.
545. **The card plan is part of the plan now: one input, one answer (Eli, Nov 14: "have it be wired to the budget, wired to everything... one correct answer, one correct output, one correct input from where the budget is").** js/cardplan.js (cardPlanOf) reads the household plan and works out the card plan; cards.html and Net worth > Credit cards both call it, so the sentence is the same in both places ("Next card move: ..." on Credit cards, with Open the card plan carrying the household in the link). Inputs and where each comes from: everyday spending from the Budget's lines by category; cards held from the Credit cards list (the library card picked on each row), each row's own Use it taps and its Opened month; debt at 8% or more from the debts (RULES.heavyApr); the score from the Profile; growth and withdrawal from the plan's settings. Taps on cards.html write back to the same places: a credit's Use it goes on the card's row (cardUse, the same taps Credit cards shows) and the opening month on the row (opened). What the plan has no place for (a business, what a Chase point is worth, programs you would use, other cards opened, credits you would use on a card you do not hold yet) is kept with the journey's choices (settings.start.cardPlan), so the link carries it. Nothing is kept in the browser alone any more. Stand-ins, each labelled and each dropped the moment the plan has the real thing: typical everyday amounts (card-offers.json typicalSpend) while the Budget has no lines in those categories, a tapped card-spending amount, and a tapped score band while the Profile has no score. Checked: 12 tests in test/cardplan.test.js (the loop test builds a plan, adds Budget lines, a card row, a score and a heavy debt, and watches the answer follow); a browser run on an empty plan and on a sample plan at 1440, 1280 and 390, light and dark, with no errors, no sideways scroll and nothing clipped; a tap on a held card's credit changed the yearly check and reopening the link kept it; the Demo client's Credit cards screen shows the sentence. Not checked: tools/e2e/layout.cjs (it did not finish in this workspace on the last batch and was not rerun), the Credit cards screen by eye at three widths with the new line, and a think-aloud with Alexis and Tom. Open: (a) someone with no cards on their plan cannot add one on cards.html; they add it under Credit cards. Fine for a coach session, a gap for a visitor who starts here. (b) The score bands (670 and 740) and the typical amounts are from review sites and my stand-ins, kept in card-offers.json with that said. (c) Welcome offers are still Chase only.
546. **Add a card you hold, on the card plan (Eli, Nov 14: "fix that").** A visitor who starts on cards.html with no cards on their plan can now add one there: tap the bank, tap the card, answer "do you pay it in full each month?". It makes the same row Net worth > Credit cards makes when a card is picked (the library card, its fee, its rewards kind, the issuer as the institution), so it shows on Credit cards too. "Yes, in full" is recorded on the row; a balance is never invented. "No, I carry a balance" turns the answer to "Pay down the debt first" until the balance is on the debts list, with a button to add it on When am I debt free? and one to say the balance is gone. Each held card also has Take this card off my plan. Checked: a test, and a browser run from an empty plan at 1440, 1280 and 390, light and dark (no errors, nothing off the edge); reopening the link kept both cards and the answer. Not checked: tools/e2e/layout.cjs and a think-aloud. Call made: a card balance someone says they carry counts as debt at 8% or more without asking the rate, since a card that charges less is rare. Say so if that should ask instead.
547. **The card plan, easier to follow: six steps and one pulse that never stops (Eli, Nov 14: "make this better and easier to understand and use, keep all the pulse ideas").** Carried over from the plan's own pulse (515): one next step at a time, worked out once (js/math/cardplan.js cardSteps, read by cardPlanOf), counted ("Step 3 of 6"), and it pulses wherever it shows. The thing to tap pulses; while that is out of view the button in the top bar pulses instead, so there is always exactly one pulse to follow, and after every tap it moves to the next thing. Hide turns it off and Show my next step brings it back; less-motion devices get a still outline (the existing st-pulse rule). The steps: add the cards you hold (or tap I hold no cards), card spending, credit score, when each card was opened (or Older than two years), open your next move, check it against one plain card. The Budget and the Profile answer steps on their own when they have the number. Also: a plain "why" sentence under the answer, three moves shown first, the fine-tune questions folded away, and Disney offers kept out of the moves until Disney is tapped as a program. Checked: a test for the steps; a browser walk through all six steps at 390, 1280 and 1440, light and dark, with a pulse to follow at every stage, no errors, nothing off the edge, nothing clipped. Not checked: tools/e2e/layout.cjs, and a think-aloud with real people.
548. **The second engine (Run 1, Nov 14 2026): what it found, what was fixed, and four things to decide.** Built: tools/audit (cases.mjs, engine2.py, run.mjs), test/audit.test.js, docs/AUDIT.md. Fixed: the lifetime plan's tax now settles to 50 cents each year (it stopped after eight rounds; 5% of free years were off, by up to $4,910); a required withdrawal and a conversion are no longer counted twice; Start at the finish line grows money at the plan's own pace. To decide: (a) Loan interest: show the formula's number (today, spec M32) or what stepping every month really pays (95 cents less on average, up to $18.80)? Default: unchanged. Suggested: the stepped number, since it is what is paid. (b) At a line where the premium credit stops and nothing settles, the plan counts no credit that year (14 of 10,339 made-up years). Default: that. (c) Roth growth taken before 59 and a half is not counted as income, and money taken from the buckets in a working year pays no income tax. Default: left out and said on the engine. Suggested: add both in Run 2 with the one-engine work. (d) The lifetime plan adds a year's saving at the start of the year; the plan's FI date adds it month by month. They can name different years for the same person. Default: unchanged until the one-engine work in Run 2 picks one.
549. **The card plan: nine more ideas, the checks, and what a think-aloud walk caught (Eli, Nov 14: "do em all and do the checks").** Built: (1) Why not the others: every card that is not a move, grouped under the one thing holding it back. (2) A way in from the plan: My card plan in the self-serve plan's footer, carrying the plan. It is not a numbered FOO step: the step count is fixed at 18 and tested, so adding one is Eli's call. (3) Fee dates on the year view: already wired, since js/year.js reads a card row's annual fee and Opened month, and the card plan writes both; a test now holds it. (4) The coach's one-pager carries the same Next card move sentence. (5) Since last time: the answer as it stood at the end of the last day you came is kept, and the page says what changed (the move, a slot back or used, an offer that ended). (6) How much of what is there your cards already give, as a percent, with "close enough to stop here" at nine tenths (card-offers.json enoughShare). (7) Two people: a plan with a Partner on any row gets a Mine / My partner's switch; each person has their own five slots, their own held cards and their own taps. (8) Where would it go: an offer's dollars against the $1,000 first cushion (RULES.littleE), this year's Roth IRA limit, or 30 years invested. (9) First card? A practice run (data/library/card-drill.json), six steps. Also: an offer past its end date drops out of the moves on its own. The walk, as Alexis and as Tom on a phone, each with a task, caught four real things, all fixed and tested: an empty plan named a card before knowing about debt (it now starts "If you owe nothing on cards:"); after "I carry a balance" the steps went on asking about spending (debt is now the only step, with See when I am debt free); a card whose fee outruns its rewards showed as "$0, 0%" (it now says what it costs); and the yearly check told a Sapphire Reserve holder to switch to the Disney Visa (it now names the no-fee card in the same points family, or says "a no-fee version" when it cannot know). Checks: node --test passes (17 card plan tests); tools/e2e/layout.cjs now has the card page's own walk (LAYOUT_ROUTES=cards: empty, then filled with every fold open) and reports zero on all nine checks at 1440, 1280 and 390, light and dark, with no errors; Credit cards and the one-pager (Demo) and the self-serve you and plan screens (390 and 1440) also report zero. Found while checking, not mine and not fixed: the layout walk for the other standalone pages times out on debt.html at every width (its selectors no longer match that page), so those pages are not being scanned, and debt.html has 4 column flags (1 distinct) on its card dropdown. 550. **Welcome offers for every bank in the library, a rate on a carried balance, and "until retirement" (Eli's answers, Nov 15).** His answers, applied: (a) a carried balance needs a rate, or an assigned one: a card added as carried now asks about how much is owed and its rate, on the card's own row; with no rate the plan's typical card rate (25%, reference typicalRates) is used, which is how the plan already treats a blank rate, and the tap "I carry a balance" drops away once the balance is on the row. (b) Point values stay as they are; he treats a point as whatever it can be redeemed for in cash, so Amex points count at their statement credit value (0.6 cents, to check) and the other banks' points at 1 cent. (c) "Left invested" now runs to the plan's own target age (the FI age if set, else Retire by, else 65) from the plan's age; with no birth year it says so instead of guessing. (d) Bonuses stay on client screens. (e) Offers now cover all 67 library cards at 12 banks plus the 26 extra Chase cards (93 records): 26 of the other banks' cards have an offer with a dollar value, and the rest say why not (no offer found today, a first-year match with no set amount, free nights, or closed to new applicants). Each bank's rule on getting an offer twice is kept and shown (Citi 48 months and once-only, Capital One's Venture family, Amex's "you may not be eligible"). The 5 card limit now stops only Chase cards: at five, the answer is a card at another bank, and every new card still uses a slot. Bank points that can be taken as cash are never held back the way airline and hotel points are. An offer the issuer words as "as high as" says the one you are shown can be lower. Still open: (f) the 26 extra Chase cards still have no credits or benefits. (g) Amex, Citi and Capital One points are counted at cash value only; what a Chase point is worth is the one tap that lifts a value, and the others have no such tap yet. (h) Every offer is from review sites; two issuer pages only. Checks: 19 card plan tests, node --test passes, the card page's layout walk reports zero at three widths, light and dark.

(h) The layout checker's walk of the journey had been timing out since the question order changed in Run 2 (it still typed pay first); it is repaired and runs again: column 36 (6 distinct), all the home card on What if, every other check 0. **For Eli:** 1. (a): fix it (a second "No debt" writes nothing)? 2. (b): rename the step's headline when money is left each month (for example "Build your first cushion"), and should the amount be the $1,000 little e? 3. (e): should the journey ask who is in the plan? 4. Should the walk become the client tutorial next (Plain wording is written for that), or stay a tester tool?

575. **SLAM the Game, Nov 24 2026: the Strategy Engine ("Moves to try"). Math I was not sure of, and questions.** Built: features/strat.js (the loop), data/strategies.js (13 strategies on one template), presets/metros.json (29 areas and a typical US metro, each value a Guess). Math to check: (a) **Vacancy is counted two times in the house hack, as the prompt wrote it**: 5% inside the reserves and again as rent x 0.95. Keep both? (b) **Reserves are a percent of the other units' rent**, not of the rent of the whole building with your own unit. Right reading of "gross rent"? (c) **FHA insurance** is 1.75% added to the loan and 0.55% a year on the loan. The yearly rate changes with the loan size and the down payment; 0.55% is the common case. (d) **"With equity"** adds the loan paid down in month 1 (it grows each month, so this is low) and 1% a year of the price, and counts both as money kept each month. (e) **Debt-to-income** uses pay before taxes = take-home / 0.78 when the tax view has no number, and counts 75% of the other rent as income, as lenders do. (f) **"Not for you" for the house hack** reads the dial row that is set: with no row set, the game takes the highest row within reach, and says Not for you when no row is. The prompt's first rule ("the 100% row needs rents above the market") alone would rule out almost every area. Right call? (g) **"It pays me"** is set at 10% of your housing cost now, paid to you. Another number? (h) **Balance transfer: the interest saved** is the interest the card you have would charge over the 0% months with the same payment, less the fee. **The room for the payment** is free-to-spend plus what you pay the card now. "Not for you" is when even the 25% row is out of reach, or the balance clears in 2 months. (i) **The FI date** comes from the game's month-by-month run, two times. A one-time gain of an interest move comes off the costly debt; any other one-time gain goes to invested money; a one-time cost is paid from the next months. (j) **A raise** uses the same tax share as now, so the take-home figure is a little high. (k) **The 0% purchase card** from savings is worth amount x 7% x months / 12 / 2 (half the cash is still invested on average). (l) **Bank bonuses** take about 1.5 hours each: the game's own estimate. Questions: (m) the career atlas and the city atlas are not in the game, so the raise is your own tap and the cheaper city compares rent only. Port them? (n) A listing is typed on the keypad. Paste is not built. Needed? (o) The credits are my best match for who taught each move (for example the CFPB for consolidation, Doctor of Credit for bank and card bonuses). Change any? (p) The metro numbers are my rough figures, not from a data file. Do you want a sourced file? (q) Each strategy has a fixed effort number from 1 to 5 that I set. Review them?

Contact Eli." until email works (the switch `EMAIL_READY` in `game/account/supa.js`). **Still open:** (a) the line that confirms you own stresslessaboutmoney.com and can sign in to its DNS arrived empty. Until you confirm it, Part 2 of the steps cannot start.

583. **RUN Launch ready, Part 1: a stable public site. What you have to do, what I found, and what I chose.** (a) **One thing only you can do: switch GitHub Pages to the branch `release`** (Settings, Pages, Branch: release, folder: / (root), Save). The clicks are in `docs/RELEASE.md`. The public link stays the same. After the switch the site only changes when a promote runs (`bash tools/promote.sh`, at the end of every run). Until you switch, the site still follows `main` as before. (b) **Found and repaired: in a browser, each row of the game's Menu closed again at once** (Glossary, What's built, Flag this screen, Where am I and the others), and the rows Theme and Check in went back to the tab before. The cause was the game's own step back in the browser history, which arrived after the new sheet was open. It was like this on `main` before this part, so it is on the public site today until a promote with this repair is live. Checked in a browser at 390 and 1280 wide: all ten rows now stay open. (c) **I did not add the rule "promote at the end of every run" to `CLAUDE.md`**, because a worker should not change the house rules on its own. It is in `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/RELEASE.md` and `STATUS.md`. Do you want this one line in `CLAUDE.md` too: "At the end of every run: `bash tools/promote.sh`, and say its last line in the summary"? (d) **"Coming soon" does not cover** the stand-alone calculator pages (`afford.html`, `cards.html` and the others), `game/admin.html` and `game/coach.html`: each has its own small script and no shared router. Say if you want the card there too. (e) **The add-ons:** each tap of the game now goes through one function. A button of an add-on that has no action in the game's action list did nothing before and shows "Coming soon" now. I cannot run the private add-on tests here; please run them one time. (f) **A late error of an action that waits** (sign-in, save to the account) shows no card, because it is most often the network and not a part that is not built. It is recorded, and the console still shows it. (g) **Optional, one click for you:** on GitHub, a branch rule for `release` ("no force pushes", "no deletion") would make it impossible to break the public site by hand. The script never forces, so the rule does not get in its way. (h) **The promote on the build machine skips the few spreadsheet tests that need ExcelJS and LibreOffice**, as every run there does. GitHub runs them on each push to `main` (the green check next to the commit).

584. **RUN Compare the ways: what I chose, what is switched off, and what to confirm.** (a) **VPW is switched off.** The Bogleheads page would not open from the build machine, so I could not check its table. The method is built from what I recall (stocks grow 5.0%, bonds 1.9% after inflation, last payment at 99) and it does give the two numbers a search showed on Nov 25 2026 (age 40: 3.6% at 40% stocks, 4.1% at 60%). To switch it on: open bogleheads.org/wiki/Variable_percentage_withdrawal, confirm those three numbers, and remove the `off` line of `age-vpw` in `js/math/withdraw.js` (then `node tools/game_withdraw_build.mjs`). (b) **The RMD method is switched off for a stop before 73.** The IRS Uniform Lifetime Table starts at 73 and irs.gov would not open to source another table. It runs for a stop at 73 or later. Do you want it to use the Single Life table for younger ages once that can be sourced? (c) **"Keep the odds in a band" (Kitces) is in the game only.** It is the retirement engine's own rule, and the web app does not carry that engine. The web row says so. (d) **Random markets are the real years in a random order** (1,000, fixed seed), in both places. The Monte Carlo of the retirement engine cannot run most of these ways (timing, smoothing, CAPE, buckets), and one test for all rows keeps them comparable. The game's Retirement odds still uses the engine's Monte Carlo, so its count and this screen's count differ. OK? (e) **Guardrails is the rule the lifetime plan already had**: the two guardrails of Guyton and Klinger (cut 10% at 20% over, raise 10% at 20% under), with a cut never below 70% of what life costs. Their rule that skips the inflation raise and their rule for the last 15 years are not in it. The game's Retirement odds uses the engine's own guardrails, which is a little different. Should one replace the other? (f) **What "works" means.** A start year works when the money lasts and no year pays under a share of what the way aims to pay: 99.9% for steady spending, 70% for guardrails, 85% for floor and ceiling (90% for the 90/110 setting), 75% for the rest. These are the numbers the lifetime plan already used. A share of the portfolio cannot run out, so its count is only "never under 75% of year one", which makes it look low. Is 75% the line you want? (g) **Steady spending and a share of the portfolio set what you spend.** Social Security pays part of it, so less comes out once it starts, and tax is on top. The spend-by-age ways (Die With Zero, VPW, RMD) set what leaves the accounts: tax comes out of it and Social Security adds to it, because they are built to use the money up. (h) **Cash earns 0% after inflation** in the bucket ways (it keeps pace with prices). The market file has no cash series. Another number? (i) **The yield shield uses the yield of the plain index** (S&P dividends and the 10-year bond). The tilt to higher-yield funds that Millennial Revolution describes is not modeled. (j) **The bond tent** is 20 points fewer stocks on the day work stops, back to your mix over 10 years. My setting; say if you want another. (k) **CAPE rule: 1.75% plus half of 1/CAPE** (Big ERN, Part 18 and Part 54). For year one on a smooth market it uses the last CAPE on file (January 2022: 36.94), which is old. (l) **"Years with a cut" is counted in the hardest start year**, and "deepest cut" looks only at start years where the money lasted (running out is counted in the odds, not as a cut). (m) **The age work can stop** is the first age where a smooth market works, year one pays what your life costs, and enough real start years work: 95% in the web app (the plan's own target), 90% in the game (its own target). Two targets because the two already differed. (n) **The game's rows have no tax** (its replay never had), and the web app's have. So the same person sees different numbers in the two places. (o) **Spreadsheet.** The game: a section in the plan spreadsheet. The web app: the lifetime plan is not in the household workbook at all (M9U is app-only), so the table downloads as its own CSV file, and the new rule numbers are on the Assumptions list. The spec's Math tab was not given a new M number: the rules are listed under M9U and M9V. Want its own M row? (p) **The market history file changed shape**: each year now also has the CAPE and two yields (same source, fetched Nov 25 2026). The returns are untouched; the tool checks all 152 before it writes.

585. **RUN Not built, groups 1 to 5, Part 1, follow-up: three small things to check.** (a) **"3 weeks sooner":** the dates are worked out in whole months, so the card says "New route: 3 months sooner" (in the game the FI date moves in whole years). Do you want weeks? That needs the plans to count in days. (b) **Health and childcare in the game:** the game's spending has five parts and none is health or childcare. So the triage card asks for the two amounts by taps, takes health out of "Bills and subscriptions" and childcare out of "Fun and everything else", and says so in the math. Is that the right place to take them from? Copays, dental and the rest of the Kids lines stay in "the rest". (c) **The "Coming soon" card** for the phone scripts is the shared one that landed on main the same day (`js/ui/soon.js`, `game/core/soon.js`). That card was made for a whole screen, so I added one small part to each file (`soonPart`: the same tag and calm words, with a title and one line, and no button) for a section inside a screen. Nothing for you to do.

586. **RUN Launch ready, Part 2: Report a bug or idea. What you have to do, and what I chose, to check.** (a) **One thing to do:** open the Supabase SQL Editor (the same project as the game's sign-in), paste the whole file `supabase/migrations/20261125190000_reports.sql` and run it one time. It is marked NOT APPLIED and is safe to run two times. I tried it two times on a throwaway PostgreSQL 16 on this machine, with each kind of visitor; I did not run it on the live database. Until you run it, a report says "Saved on this device" and waits there; after, the waiting reports send themselves the next time the person opens the app, and the admin section "Reports inbox" fills. (b) **A visitor who is not signed in** (the sign-in screen, the welcome screen, each page of the web app) can send a report and can read nothing, not even their own. Such a report has no owner, so there is nobody to tell when you mark it Fixed. To keep the table safe with an open door, the database takes at most 100 reports an hour from all visitors together and 30 an hour from one account. Are those ceilings right? (c) **The picture** is chosen by the person (a file or the camera; nothing takes a picture of the screen by itself), made small in the browser (at most 900 pixels, about 150 KB at most) and stored inside the report row as text. I made no storage bucket. Only the person and you can see it. Is a picture inside the row fine for the soft launch, or do you want a bucket later? While a report waits on the device, at most 3 pictures are kept there. (d) **The app version.** The game: one short code made from the content stamps of its scripts (for example `game-3c201e26`); it changes when any script changes, and the Report sheet shows it under "What goes with it". The web app: the day the page was published (for example `web-2026-11-25`). Neither is a git hash. If `tools/promote.sh` should write one date for both apps at promote time, say so and I will switch. (e) **The web app shell already has a tab named Report** (the client's report), so its button says "Report a bug or idea" in full; the game and the other pages say "Report". (f) **Where the button sits.** The game: in the top bar of each tab (on a phone, on the row of "Where am I"), at the end of each sheet, under the welcome card and inside the sign-in card. At the end of a sheet means a long sheet needs a scroll to reach it; the header row of a sheet was already full on a phone. Do you want it in the header there instead, in place of something? The coach page and the admin page have no Report button. (g) **Privacy of the taps.** The game sends the code names of the last 3 buttons (for example `safe`, `tab`). The pages that stand alone send the words on the button with every digit taken out. The web app shell sends code names only, because a button there can show a client's name. The note is the person's own words and is sent as typed. (h) **Flag this screen and Report this screen are folded in.** The old flags stay readable in the game under Me, "Your reports"; the old notes of the web app stay in Coach tools and in the export. New reports no longer go into the client file or the copied game: they go to the inbox. (i) **Open their game at this screen** opens the tab and the sheet by its name. A sheet that needs a row or an amount to open (one debt, one goal) opens at its list, not at the same row, because a report never holds a value. (j) **Download everything** (the backup of the admin page) does not include the reports yet: that function is part of the file already applied, and I did not change it.
