What was checked instead: every formula uses only functions on a list that Google Sheets has (`test/firesheet.test.js`, 42 functions), and LibreOffice works every formula out with no error cell (the FIRE sheet in 8 cases, the game's whole workbook, the web app's Demo workbook). Please do it once: in the game, Menu, Export, download the file, then in Google Sheets: File, Import, Upload. Tell me any cell that shows an error, and whether the blue inputs and the shaded "Stop work" row look right. (b) **The blank FIRE sheet link was not opened either** (https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1HZBHzEeDgQRmGcqwzaXW1jgd2N3SPSddZkhS7svZhHY/copy). It is written once, in `game/features/firelink.js`. Please tap it once while signed out of SLAM. (c) **The FIRE sheet is a simple model on purpose**, so that every cell can be a formula: no income tax, one invested balance, one growth rate a year. So its balance differs from the app's plan. The app's own withdrawals and balance sit beside it in two grey columns, as values, with a note. Is that the layout you want, or do you want one tax rate on withdrawals as one more blue input? (d) **Income is not the same thing in the two apps.** The web app's sheet uses pay before tax (that is what its lifetime plan holds); the game's uses take-home pay. So the savings rate is on a different base in each. Each label says which. Tell me if you want one base. (e) **The FIRE sheet tab does not come back in.** It is for trying numbers. Changes come back from the plan's own tabs (the web app) or from "Your numbers" on the tab "My plan" (the game). Each file says so on the tab. Want the stop age and the spending of the FIRE sheet read back too? (f) **Which ways to take money out are live formulas**: the same amount each year, a share of the balance, a floor and a ceiling, and guardrails (the web app's rule: 20% bands, 10% steps, never under 70%). Any other way, and any way added later, keeps the live column on "the same amount each year", says so on the sheet, and shows the app's own numbers for that way beside it. The game's guardrails are the engine's Guyton-Klinger rule, a little different from the formula; the game's own values are in the grey columns. (g) **The game's sheet**: growth is the game's 7% a year, Social Security starts at 67, and a pension or other income is not in the live columns (they are in the game's own values). The grey columns need the retirement engine to be ready; a file made before that has no grey columns and no engine tabs. (h) **The .xlsx button needs a connection** in the game: the game's service worker keeps nothing, and the spreadsheet tool (ExcelJS) comes from a public CDN, as in the web app. With no connection the screen says so and the CSV still works. Want ExcelJS put in the repository (about 1 MB) so the file also works offline? (i) **The menu.** The game's row "My plan as a spreadsheet" is now "Export" (same screen, one row). The web app has "Export" in the More menu; the buttons on Coach tools, Data stay. The self-serve journey (start.html) and the small calculators have no workbook, so no Export entry. (j) **The landing screen's "What is this?" does not exist yet**, so the blank FIRE sheet button is only in the two Export entries. The screen can call `fireBlankButton()`. (k) **The web app's stop age on the sheet** is the age picked on the Lifetime plan, else "Retire by" (65 when not set). The age "history would have carried" (the Lifetime screen's own pick when you chose none) is not used on the sheet: that search takes too long for a download. Want it anyway? (l) The spec's Math tab has a new row, M9I (the FIRE sheet), and the export format is one version up.

Sign in to admin.html with the fresh account: it says "Not the admin". One more by-hand point about the web app is in the run summary and not here, by the rule that no add-on word goes in the repository. So today the button leaves them, and the page says so. `delete_my_account()` removes them. If you delete a sign-in by hand on the admin page, the reports of that person stay, with no owner. 590. **RUN Launch ready, Part 6: the front door. The link, what I chose, and what to check.** (a) **The link for the slide is https://sapphirestoneage.github.io/Slam/game/** and its QR code is `docs/qr-slam.png`. It is the address the game already has, so old links keep working. A new person sees the landing screen; you (signed in) see your game. (b) **"Am I okay?" did not exist as a question with an answer, so I built the smallest one.** It adds no new math. It shows what the person tapped (could they pay a $1,000 surprise, how long the cushion lasts, do they invest, which debts) and three things the game already works out from those taps: the months of cushion, the money left from each paycheck (typical pay minus typical spending for people like them), and the era. Every line says Guess and there is no grade. That is a place to start from, and it has a first step." Are those three sentences the tone you want? I took the "See my first plan" shortcut off this path because the answer needs those taps. My guess is 1.5 to 2.5 minutes for a real person; a robot cannot time reading. Please time two real people. If it runs long, the fix is to show the answer right after the gut checks (5 taps) and finish the setup after: say so and it is a small change. (d) **A game made before the account goes into the account at sign-up.** If the person instead signs in to an account that already has a game, the game from this phone is added to that account as one more person (the existing merge rule). Is that what you want, or should it ask first? (e) **With email confirmation on, the person leaves to their mail app and comes back through the link.** The game on the phone waits and is saved to the account on return, in the same browser. If they open the mail link in a different browser, that browser has no game and they start again. (f) **The blank FIRE sheet button** on the landing screen is the shared one of the Export run (`fireBlankButton()` in `game/features/firelink.js`), so the words and the link are the same in each place. (g) **The privacy page** of Part 4 is linked inside "What is this?", next to the advice line (both come from the one place Part 4 keeps them). (h) **Not checked:** the real sign-up and sign-in against Supabase (the build machine cannot reach it; the walk uses a stand-in), a real phone camera on the QR picture (it was read back by software only), and iPhone Safari.

592. **RUN Live Session Mode, Parts 1 and 2: the invite link and the session room. What you have to do, and what I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **One database file to run, when the whole build is done or before a dry run:** `supabase/migrations/20261125210000_live_sessions.sql` (NOT APPLIED; paste it in the Supabase SQL Editor and press Run, one time; safe to run two times). It makes four tables (invite links, the tag "coaching client of this coach", sessions, transcript lines as text) and seven functions. A session, its invite and its lines are readable by that coach, that client and you (the admin), not by every coach account. I also added four lines to `20261125200000_delete_my_account.sql` (not applied yet either), so Delete my account removes these rows too. (b) **Realtime: nothing to switch on if your project has the default settings.** The two devices talk through one Realtime channel named by the session id (Broadcast and Presence), with the public key and the person's own sign-in. No secret, no table in a publication. One thing to look at one time: Supabase dashboard, Realtime, Settings, "Allow public access" must be ON (it is ON by default). I could not check your dashboard from here. If the link is off, the room says so and each device still saves its own lines and reads the room again each 30 seconds. Free plan limits I assumed from memory, not re-read today: 200 devices connected at one time, 100 messages a second, 2 million a month. Please look under Usage after the first real call. (c) **Everything is behind one switch that is off:** `LIVE_SESSION_READY = false` in `game/account/liveflag.js`. Until the last part passes, the coach page shows "Coming soon" where the two buttons will be, and the page `game/session.html` shows "Coming soon". (d) **Nothing here was tried with a real microphone, two real devices, real speech recognition or the real realtime channel.** That cannot be done from the build machine. The tests run two simulated devices with scripted lines. The list of what a real dry run must check is in `docs/LIVE-SESSION-BUILD.md`. The biggest unknown: does Chrome start listening on the client's laptop when you press Start, with no tap on their side? If it refuses, their light says Stopped and Reconnect is one tap. (e) **The brief says red for "stopped". The app has a no-red rule, so "stopped" is a plain outline circle with the word "Stopped" and a Reconnect button.** Green is working, amber is restarting. Say if you want red here as the one exception. (f) **The invite screen has one thing more than name, email, password: a second button "I already have an account. Sign in".** Without it a person who already has an account, or who confirms the email on a different device, could not get in. OK? (g) **The coach's name on the invite screen ("Dana invited you to SLAM").** The coach page fills it in from the first part of the coach's email and you can change it one time in the box "Your name, as your client sees it"; it is remembered on that device. OK, or do you want a name kept with the coach's account? (h) **"Start session" for a person already on your roster** opens the room for you and them. Today the roster is every account that shares its game with a coach (question 589 (c)), so a coach account could open a room with any of them. The room stays empty unless that person opens the session page and taps OK, and they are tagged as that coach's client only when they join. OK for now? The existing client has to open `game/session.html` to join; a button for that inside the game is not built yet (which part should add it?). (i) **End asks one time first** ("End the session for both of you?" with "Keep going"), because End cannot be undone: there is one Start for the whole call. Both sides can Pause and End. OK? (j) **The microphone.** The page never opens the microphone itself and records nothing. The browser's own speech recognition asks for it. In Chrome that service sends the sound to Google to turn it into words; SLAM receives words only. This is how the Web Speech API works; the privacy text (Part 9) should say it. OK to go on with it? (k) **The echo filter numbers are a first setting, to tune after a real call.** Normal: a line is dropped when nearly the same words came from the other device in the last 2 seconds; "nearly the same" is the same words after lowercasing and removing punctuation, or 80% shared words; a line of 1 or 2 words is never dropped (two people both say "okay"). When a side taps "No headphones": 4 seconds, 60%, lines of 2 words or more, and half a sentence inside the whole sentence counts. Because of this a line goes to the database 2 seconds (4 with no headphones) after it was heard; it is safe on the device from the first moment. (l) **The link in the confirmation email.** If Supabase does not have `.../game/session.html` in its list of allowed redirect addresses, the email link opens the game, and the game sends the person on to the session page (the invite is kept on the device). It works either way on the same device. To make it direct: Supabase dashboard, Authentication, URL Configuration, add `https://sapphirestoneage.github.io/Slam/game/session.html`. (m) **Safari.** The brief lists Safari as a browser that cannot listen. Newer Safari has a speech recognizer of its own. The room shows "Their side can't listen in this browser" only where the browser has none, so on a new iPhone it may try to listen. Should Safari and phones always be treated as "cannot listen"? (n) Still open from the coordinator: "I'm on a paid Zoom plan: YES / NO".

What I chose, for your yes or no, and what nobody has seen yet.** (a) **Nobody here saw what Chrome's speech recognition really writes.** Does it write "eighteen fifty" as 1850, "$1,850" or "18 50"? "percent" or "%"? Does it cut a sentence in two lines? I wrote the reader for the forms listed in `docs/LIVE-SESSION-BUILD.md` and tested each one with typed lines. A real call has to check them (items 14 to 20 of the dry-run list there). When the reader is not sure it sets nothing: the line is kept for your review. (b) **The rules are done, but they are not joined to a real saved game yet.** The session page holds no game. Part 6 (the two screens) has to load the client's game there; then one call (`lfAttach`) switches the fill on. Until then nothing fills on the real page. The switch is still off. (c) **Only one device writes: the client's.** It holds their game, hears their words first-hand, and tells your screen. So if the client is on a phone that cannot listen, nothing fills live (your words never fill). The AI pass after the call is what fills such a call. OK? (d) **The numbers I picked:** a question of yours stays open 20 seconds; a new number in 8 seconds is a correction; the client has 10 seconds to answer a "Confirming"; a yes that repeats a word you said in the last 3 seconds counts as an echo. Change any of them? (e) **"A Guess that the person typed themselves".** The game has no mark for that: every number a person saves is already "real" (Close). The one case that looks like the game's own guess: a person types an amount in "one small question at a time" and taps "I don't know yet". The game itself re-scales such a row today, so the call may set it too; the amount from before the call is kept for the review. Should a typed "I don't know yet" amount be protected from the call (it needs one new mark in the game)? (f) **A rough number that you confirm out loud stays a Guess.** "400-ish, maybe 500" lands as 450. If you then say "Confirming groceries 450" and they say yes, the list says confirmed but the level stays Guess and it stays in the review, because the 450 was never said by them. A different, firm number ("Confirming groceries 480", yes) becomes Close. Is that what you meant by "rough numbers never Close", or should any yes make it Close? (g) **What counts as a yes:** confirmed, yes, yep, yeah, yup, correct, right, that's right. NOT okay, sure, uh huh (people say them to show they listen). A no: no, nope, nah, actually, wait, not quite, not exactly, hold on. Add or remove a word? (h) **Rent, utilities and one line of the game.** The game has one line "Home" for rent plus utilities, "Food" for groceries plus eating out, and so on. The call adds the pieces it hears (rent 1,850 + utilities 150 = Home 2,000) and the line is Close only when each piece heard was confirmed. OK? (i) **Income.** The game keeps one month, after tax. "2,100 each paycheck, every two weeks" becomes 2,100 x 26 / 12 = 4,550. An amount "a year" is NOT set (a yearly salary is before tax): it is kept for the review. An amount with no "a month" or "each paycheck" is kept too. When you confirm income, say "a month" or "each paycheck". OK? (j) **Not built: monthly debt payments and the mortgage.** The game cannot hold a payment as a Guess, and has no debt line for a mortgage (a mortgage payment said as "rent or mortgage" lands in Home). Also: the game has ONE line for all credit cards, so two cards cannot be two balances. Card names you hear (Chase, Discover) are kept for the review and set nothing. Do you want one line for each card? That is a change of the game, not of this feature. (k) **What-ifs: the cue words** are if, what if, worst case, best case, suppose, thinking about, let's say, hypothetically, someday, one day, I wish, hoping to, dream, in case, would love to, would like to, want to, and maybe / perhaps / possibly / would / could / might. "Want to" and "would" may catch too much; a real call will show. After YOUR what-if question, the client's next numbers are what-ifs too (20 seconds, or until your next plain question). (l) **Where a typed what-if goes:** Ceiling and Floor keep the limit next to the field (nothing in the game shows a limit yet: which screen should?). Scenario links to the simulator that fits the words, and adds no life event block by itself. Life transition links to "Break in case of emergency" (one transition today, a layoff). Goal or wish adds one goal to the bucket list with the cost from the quote, or the game's typical cost for that kind. OK? (m) **Three small changes in the game itself, so a heard Guess keeps its amount:** the fill of a money area no longer re-scales or removes a line that a call set; setup run again keeps it; a debt's rate can now be a Guess with a value (before, a typed rate was always Exact). A person who never has a live session sees no difference; the tests of the game pass.

599. **RUN Money sins, Part 1: the read. What I chose, what could not be built, for your yes or no.** (a) **The words are mine**: fifteen quiz moments, the money meaning and the light side of each of the 7 sins and the 12 ways of refusing change, and 14 of the 19 defenses (yours are lust: the cooling-off timer, envy: is this yours or theirs?, sloth: automate plus one number a week, panic: the 72-hour rule, greed: you have already won). All in `js/quiz/skins/sins.js`; please read and change any line. (b) **Pride is two traits**: `pride` (the pull: spends to look the part) and `proud`, shown as "Pride (digging in)" (the refusal: digs in when the plan says change course). Is "Pride (digging in)" the name you want? (c) **The numbers I set** (in `SN_RULES`, js/math/sins.js): evidence counts half after 30 days (a month is the rhythm money runs on; a stopped habit fades to a quarter in a season); the quiz counts in full alone and shares out as the rest comes in (weight Q / (Q + E)); a label needs 2 sources that agree AND 30 days of ledger; the check-in line starts at $50 (taps $25 to $500); a pattern: 3 or more buys from payday to 2 days after, 12 food buys or 8 fun buys in a month, a category over its plan by 10% and $25, no new money data for 45 days, the app closed 21 days, half the main numbers still a Guess after a month, a Recalculating card waiting 30 days; a push-back is 3 points; a defense that worked adds 3, one that held takes 2. Change any of them? (d) **"Planned" adds nothing**, "Stressed" points at Panic and Wrath, "Someone asked" at People-pleasing. OK? (e) **Signals NOT built because the data is not kept** (nothing faked): *late night*: a buy has a day, never an hour (a game buy keeps the day it was logged; a bank CSV has the date only); it would need the hour of the buy from the bank, or a "when was it?" tap. *A stress tag*: none existed; the "stressed" check-in tap is used as one. *The Safe to spend trade-off card accepted or skipped*: neither app keeps the tap; it would need { at, choice } saved on each answer. *Opening or ignoring a Recalculating card*: not kept; the game uses what is kept (the day the new date was kept, c.x.rc.base.at, and whether a card is showing); the web app has neither (it would need the day a card is shown and the day it is kept). *Changing everything after a bad month*: no log of edits by month in the game; it would need a count of number edits per month. *Postponing fire drills*: only drills done are kept (c.x.drills); a drill run counts as evidence against Fear and Panic. *Going back to old numbers*: no undo log in the game; the web history could be read later. *In the web app*: the app left closed and numbers at Guess are not read yet (no visit log in the self-serve file). (f) **The web app has no "log a buy" moment**, only the statement import, so its check-ins are on the read itself ("What was this?", up to 3 imported buys that pass the line or trip a pattern). In the game the check-in comes right after Cash flow > Money out, before the Logged screen. OK? (g) **One defense at a time**, for the top sin first; it moves to the next most likely read in that family when the pattern held. Should the refusals get their own month at the same time? (h) **Privacy**: Me only by default. In the web app it is left out of the coach's screens, a partner's view and every shared copy until "Show my read" is on, and even then the check-ins and push-backs stay home. In the game a coach or an admin sees "Kept private by the player" until the player turns it on. The quiz's own "Show it on my character sheet" still only shows the line "Main pull". (i) **The judge** (run 2 Part 10) is not on main: each push-back is kept in the read (`sn.push`) and `snJudgeLog` gives the judge its record. (j) **"That is me" on the sins quiz card is a push-back**, not an Exact: the read is "Confirmed by you" only when you said yes AND two sources agree over a month. (k) **No spec row**: the read stays in the app (settings.start.lenses.sins, c.x.qz.sins); the functions are support functions in js/registry/math.js. (l) **For Parts 2 and 3**: the archetype names, the add-on hook and the Rings theme are not started.

(f) **A job hop** counts the raise after tax invested each month, less the match that stays with the job (from the Vesting card) off what is invested today. (g) **Lifetime value** is in today's dollars with no discount rate (the older tool had one as an option); payback and the lifetime value count from the end of the path, to the age you work until (the game uses the player's stop age). (h) **Not built**: the career and city atlases (waiting on data, as before); a credential stack in the game is by taps (a kind, a start, a length), not typed names or dates. The game's freedom date and the web app's FI date are each app's own (the game's goalSim, the web's M53), so the two can differ for the same move, as they already do for the FI date itself.

Plain A to Z would put Betterment, a robo-advisor, first, which your prompt rules out. The screen says the order and "Nothing here is picked for you." OK? (c) **The trade-offs are plain words, with no numbers** (for example "Safe and easy to reach, but it grows slowly. The rate can change."). Are these fair to each kind? (d) **The amount presets** are the goal's own date (when it has one), the current pace, then 6 months, 1, 2 and 3 years, each worked out by the reverse of the Time-Money dial (what is left ÷ the months, rounded up). They count no interest. (e) **Each deposit is rounded up to a dollar** (for example $300 a month paid every 2 weeks is $139 each payday, so a little more than $300 goes in a month). OK, or round to the nearest dollar? (f) **"I set it up" is the exit Automate it of the deposit**, on the person's word: SLAM cannot see the bank. The check-in every 3 months starts from the day of the wiring. (g) **The wired amount becomes the countdown's monthly amount**, so the goal has one number a month; unwire takes it back out. (h) **The open enrollment check and "Is my coverage still right?" are in a new season, Open enrollment month (Nov 1 to 30)**, as your prompt says "Nov". That is SLAM's reminder month, not a rule. No Marketplace window is shown: the repository has no dated source for it, so the person taps the last day of their own window. Do you want the Marketplace dates shown, with a source you send? (i) **The dates are two taps** (the month, then the closest of 1, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25 or the last day), not a typed date. The reminder lands on Today 21 days before the open enrollment window closes, 45 days before the FSA deadline, and 30 days before each renewal. These lead times are my choice. (j) **HSA receipts are a seasonal item of tax season** ("Save your HSA receipts"), next to the tax forms. The EOB check starts when the person taps "I saw a doctor"; it stays on Today until "The bill matched". (k) **A policy date makes a task of its own** for the Insurance Coordinator (so "N of M" grows by one for each date tapped), next to the general "Renew policies and compare prices". (l) **Not walked by a real person**, and `tools/e2e/layout.cjs` checks the web app screens, not the game: I changed no web app screen. The game walk was in Chromium at 390 and 1280 with a made-up player.

602. **RUN Money sins, Part 2: results and growth. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **The archetypes are made from two parts**, not 84 hand-made names: "The <way of refusing change> <main pull>", and one line "<the pull's habit>, <the refusal's habit>." The pulls are animals (Pride Peacock, Greed Dragon, Envy Chameleon, Gluttony Raccoon, Lust Magpie, Wrath Bull, Sloth Koala) and the refusals are words in front (Digging in: Dug-In, Fear: Peekaboo, Panic: Hair-Trigger, Nostalgia: Vintage, Denial: Unbothered, Procrastination: Someday, Blame: Courtroom, Magical thinking: Wishing-Well, Perfectionism: Blueprint, Helplessness: Shrugging, People-pleasing: Always-Yes, Handing it off: Passenger). All 84: Pride: Dug-In Peacock, Peekaboo Peacock, Hair-Trigger Peacock, Vintage Peacock, Unbothered Peacock, Someday Peacock, Courtroom Peacock, Wishing-Well Peacock, Blueprint Peacock, Shrugging Peacock, Always-Yes Peacock, Passenger Peacock; Greed: Dug-In Dragon, Peekaboo Dragon, Hair-Trigger Dragon, Vintage Dragon, Unbothered Dragon, Someday Dragon, Courtroom Dragon, Wishing-Well Dragon, Blueprint Dragon, Shrugging Dragon, Always-Yes Dragon, Passenger Dragon; Envy: Dug-In Chameleon, Peekaboo Chameleon, Hair-Trigger Chameleon, Vintage Chameleon, Unbothered Chameleon, Someday Chameleon, Courtroom Chameleon, Wishing-Well Chameleon, Blueprint Chameleon, Shrugging Chameleon, Always-Yes Chameleon, Passenger Chameleon; Gluttony: Dug-In Raccoon, Peekaboo Raccoon, Hair-Trigger Raccoon, Vintage Raccoon, Unbothered Raccoon, Someday Raccoon, Courtroom Raccoon, Wishing-Well Raccoon, Blueprint Raccoon, Shrugging Raccoon, Always-Yes Raccoon, Passenger Raccoon; Lust: Dug-In Magpie, Peekaboo Magpie, Hair-Trigger Magpie, Vintage Magpie, Unbothered Magpie, Someday Magpie, Courtroom Magpie, Wishing-Well Magpie, Blueprint Magpie, Shrugging Magpie, Always-Yes Magpie, Passenger Magpie; Wrath: Dug-In Bull, Peekaboo Bull, Hair-Trigger Bull, Vintage Bull, Unbothered Bull, Someday Bull, Courtroom Bull, Wishing-Well Bull, Blueprint Bull, Shrugging Bull, Always-Yes Bull, Passenger Bull; Sloth: Dug-In Koala, Peekaboo Koala, Hair-Trigger Koala, Vintage Koala, Unbothered Koala, Someday Koala, Courtroom Koala, Wishing-Well Koala, Blueprint Koala, Shrugging Koala, Always-Yes Koala, Passenger Koala. Change any part in `js/quiz/skins/sins.js` (`grow.arch`) and all its names change with it. (b) **The virtues** (mine): Humility, Contentment, Gratitude, Moderation, Patience, Calm, Diligence for the sins; Openness, Courage, Steadiness, Presence, Honesty, Momentum, Ownership, Groundedness, Progress, Agency, Self-respect, Involvement for the refusals. (c) **The Recalculating move** of the card is the read's defense of the month (Test the fix), with the next turn of a Recalculating card under it when one shows. (d) **The framing line**: the way of refusing change speaks first, then the pull, and only once that family has a label (Close or Exact); an Early read adds no line. Your four (fear: tiny first steps; panic: wait first; pride: the path you are on next to the other path; nostalgia: the old number next to today's) are in, and the other 15 are written in kind (`grow.frame`). It shows on Home, the month check and the crash drill in the game; in the web app on the Recalculating card, the short-month card and Done for this month. The web app has no crash-day screen, so no crash line there: where should it go? (e) **Couples**: in the game, two characters of the household who both turn on "Share my read with" see how their reads collide and a money date (8 prompts). In the web app, a couple's file now keeps a second read for the partner ("Taking it as" on the quiz page; kept under `settings.start.lenses.sinsPartner`, new: a file without it reads as before). Both reads use the household's one ledger, because the file does not split buys by person for the read. OK? (f) **Retake each quarter**: 91 days after the last take, a line on the read ("Retake the quiz" or "Not this quarter") and, in the game, a nudge in the Quest Log list. A retake keeps everything else of the read. Each take goes into a new history (`sn.hist`, at most 12; a read from before Part 2 gets its first entry when it is retaken or shown). The sin weakening is the share of its family's points held by the top of your first take, at each take; I call the virtue "growing" when that share goes down. OK? Part 1 had a slip I fixed: Take it again in the game wiped the whole read (check-ins, push-backs); now it keeps them. (g) **Graduation**: the game has no graduation story yet, so `sngGradStory` (game) and `snGradStory` (both apps) wait for it; the web graduation card was left as is, because the read is private and that card is the coach's: should the story show the read only when "Show my read" is on? (h) **The add-on line for the virtues**: none of its words are in this repository. The game has the hook: an add-on pushes { pk, virtues, line } into `SNG_VOICE` (features/sins.js), and the card shows those names and that line only while that add-on is on for the person; the share card never does. **The text itself must be added in the private add-on source by your add-on session.** The web app has no add-ons, so it always shows the default names. (i) The share card is words only (no number, no check-in, no push-back) and the link it carries is the plain link to the app.

603. **RUN Live Session Mode, Parts 8 and 9: the AI pass, privacy, the tests and the report. What I chose, for your yes or no, and what you do by hand.** Your list, in order, is in `docs/LIVE-SESSION-BUILD.md`, "For Eli: before the first real call". Price read on 2026-11-25: 4 dollars a million tokens in, 20 out; my guess, before any real call, is 10 to 30 cents for each pass. The review shows the token count of each pass. When the AI declines for safety reasons, the API runs the same request on another model it picks (Anthropic's "fallbacks"); say if you want that off. The reader takes "Speaker 1" on its own line or in front of the words, with or without a time, and names you gave in the recorder ("Eli 00:12"). Please send one made-up test file (no client words) if the first real one does not read well. A file of another call is flagged, not refused. (g) **Start now waits** until the client allowed the microphone and answered "Using headphones?"; your card says "Waiting for them to allow the microphone." and offers "Start with my side only". I kept your words and added one line under them: "To turn speech into words, Chrome sends the sound to Google while you talk; SLAM keeps the words only. The AI step after the call sends the words to Anthropic, and only when your coach runs it." with a link to the privacy page. OK, or other words? The admin cannot delete it from the app. If a device that was offline sends its last lines late (the database allows an hour after End), the card says so and offers to delete again. Should a delete also stop late lines for good (one more database rule)? It says "Anthropic's own terms cover what it receives" and nothing more about how long Anthropic keeps data: I did not check Anthropic's terms for your account type. (k) Still open: "I'm on a paid Zoom plan: YES / NO" (592 (n)); and the database file must run again if an older copy of it ever ran (it adds the microphone step and the delete rule; safe to run two times).

606. **RUN Finish everything partly built, Part 4: Travel. What I chose where the prompt did not say, for your yes or no.** (a) **No outside rates.** The card fee chips (0%, 1%, 2%, 3%), the visa and insurance chips and the cents-a-point chips are taps, and the page says they are not prices (your card's terms, the country's own site, the quote). No official visa fee, insurance price or card fee is in the code. (b) **One currency, no exchange rate.** A trip can be in US dollars, euros, pounds, Canadian dollars, pesos, yen or shekels, and the split uses it. Your plan is in US dollars, so a trip in another currency stays out of the true cost, the journey and the FI date (the page says so) rather than guess a rate. Do you want a typed rate, or a dated source? (c) **The stages, read this way:** Digging out = net worth (cash + invested - debt) at $0 or more; First $1k = $1,000 in cash; Debt free = no consumer debt in the web app (cards, personal, BNPL, payday, medical, family loans) and no 8%+ debt in the game (the game's own rule); $100k = net worth; Coast and FI = invested next to each app's own Coast and FI numbers. Where you are = the first stage not reached. (d) **How a trip moves you:** paid from cash first, then investments; a stage it takes you back under is named; the stages ahead move by the months of saving it costs (the trip over what you save a month, marked as an estimate); FI is the plan's own date run again (web: the cost off what is invested; game: the cost paid first from what is left each month). (e) **Group split:** by share (one share each unless tapped; a couple can be 2) or by item (each line among the people in on it), on the household split engine, in whole cents adding up (a leftover cent to the first person). No "who paid what, settle up" yet: say if you want it. (f) **The age chip** on trip.html now also puts your birth year on the plan (as the Fire drills page does), so Coast can be placed.

    **ANSWERED by Eli, Nov 26 2026 ((a), the $30k/$90k rule). Built the same day:** "30k is cutting 100; 90k is if you invested that 100." Then: the defaults are 30 years and 7% a year (a true 7%, compounded monthly), with the plan's own return as another tap. Then: "Keep it as round but basically it's 1/3." So the calculator Cut it, or invest it? (cutinvest.html, on the shelf of quick calculators, now nineteen; game: Menu > Quick math) shows the round rule in the headline (about $30k and about $90k for $100 a month, scaled by the amount) with the line "Cutting it is about 1/3 of investing it", and the exact figures in Show the math ($36,000 kept and about $117,000 invested for $100 a month over 30 years at 7%). See the new item at the end for what I chose where the answers did not say.

613. **UX pass, phase 3 (setup as a spiral, D, A, I, T, E).** (a) **Where the questions went.** D: the kinds of debt. A: the kinds of account, then the three gut checks (the $1,000 surprise, how long you could cover bills, investing). I: work, where money comes from, steady or not. T: three new taps (about how much goes to taxes, the match only for a player with a job, tax-friendly saving only for a player with such an account). E: home, who lives with you, relationship, who relies on you, combining money, then Social Security and "how money feels" (both optional). Age stays first, before the five rounds. Is the T round right, or do you want other questions there? (b) **More taps.** The front door path is now at most 40 taps, up from 25, because each round has its number and its level change. The landing says "Five short rounds of taps" in place of "About 15 taps". OK? (c) **The fast finish after page 5 is gone** for new players: "Stop here, I'll come back" at the end of each round takes its place. OK? (d) **The route math.** The money left each month goes to one stop at a time, in the order of the eras ($1,000 cushion, the match, debt at 8% or more, the full emergency fund, a year of tax-friendly filling); the later eras come from the money invested. With more going out than coming in, every date says "no date yet" and the plan says the first stop is finding that room. Is that the order you want? (e) **"Change my answers"** uses the same page order but no screens between the rounds. (f) **Desktop:** the first plan is still one column in the sheet, not side by side.

614. **RUN Not built, groups 1 to 5, Part 4: Eli's frameworks and the canon. What I chose where the prompt did not say, for your yes or no.** (a) **The words of your own frameworks are mine**: the SWAN number (months of what you must spend plus one bill you worry about), the nine steps of the Second Mouse checklist, the three Trust Framework questions with their answers and "what to ask" lines, and the Advice translator (the 14 entries of the unlearn list, read at the step you are on: fits now, fit before, for later, and the one to unlearn now). Please read them in `js/math/frameworks.js` and change any line there; both apps follow. (b) **swan.html does not save to your sleep-at-night number** on Cash layers (`profile.sleepAtNight`); it says where that number lives. Should it offer "Use this as my sleep-at-night number"? (c) **The Baby Steps are read from numbers where the plan has them**: step 1 cash of $1,000 or more; step 2 every debt but the home at $0; step 3 cash of 3 months of spending (the low end of his 3 to 6); step 4 15% of pay before taxes to retirement (web: what goes to saving and investing, less goal saving, over the pay; game: one tap); step 5 college for the kids (web: a 529 with money in it, else not known; game: one tap); step 6 the home paid off; step 7 when the rest are done. They sit next to the SLAM path on Plan › FOO (a third choice of the toggle) and in the game's Famous money ideas. OK? The moves at each stage are mine, from the book's ideas. (e) **The net worth peak** grows what you have by the plan's return after inflation plus what you add each year, and finds the first age when the Die With Zero spend-down (the same rule as Compare the ways) to your last age pays a year of spending. It counts no Social Security and no taxes; "Leave to others at the end" is a tap ($0, $100k, $250k, $500k). (f) **0% tax** is federal only, 2026: other income up to the standard deduction, then long-term gains inside the 0% band; the cash a sale gives is the gains over the share of the sale that is gain (a tap). Social Security is not counted. The older game idea now uses the same formula. (g) **Your Money or Your Life**: the one path is in the game (Menu). Step 1b opens My Money, step 2 the work hours, step 8 While you slept. Step 3 shows "Soon" until run 1 Part 21 builds it (the path calls the action `ym3` when it exists). The ideas of step 6 and step 7 are mine. Step 9 is capital, cushion and cash: what your investments pay a month at the game's 4%, a cushion of 6 months of spending, and the cash above it. The web app has steps 1a (earned.html), 2 (wage.html) and 8 (the crossover chart), but no one page for the path: do you want one? (h) **The next dollar on Home** is the FOO step you are on (web) or Which account next (game), with the match named whenever it is not the step itself. The two apps have their own orders, so they can name different steps. (i) **Quick wins**: the list of 12 is mine (the name is Brad Barrett's, ChooseFI); what each saved is the person's own tap, so no win carries a made-up amount. (j) **Time buckets and the designed week** are in the game only: decades from your age to your last age, six kinds of experience, costs as taps ($500 to $15,000), and a week of six kinds of hours next to the week you have (from your work hours and the answers of 168 hours). Not joined to the FI number. Should they be? (k) Not walked by a real person or on a phone; see the report for what was checked in a browser.

615. **RUN Finish everything partly built, Part 5: Student loans. What I chose, and what I could not check, for your yes or no.** (a) **Sources and dates.** The rate for each school year (2019-20 to 2026-27, undergraduate) comes from Federal Student Aid's rate table and the 2026-27 announcement of Jun 4 2026, read through web search on Nov 25 2026: the official pages themselves (studentaid.gov, fsapartners.ed.gov) could not be opened from here. Graduate and PLUS rates are the undergraduate rate plus 1.55% and 2.55% (the law's add-ons); I checked that against the printed graduate and PLUS rates of 2022-23, 2024-25, 2025-26 and 2026-27. Years before 2019-20 are not in the table: the person types the rate from their loan list. Do you want older years added? (b) **The PSLF employer rule** (from July 1 2026, an employer with a "substantial illegal purpose" can be dropped) is said in plain words with its status: set aside by a federal court in July 2026, the case still going on Aug 31 2026 (a law firm update and the Congressional Research Service). I could not confirm whether it was appealed after that. Please check before you rely on it. (c) **No capitalization when payments start**: since July 1 2023 the interest from school and the grace period waits beside the principal on a Direct Loan (read through Tate Law, not the regulation itself). The stepladder takes each year's loan as paid out in September and school as ending in May; payments start after the 6-month grace period unless the person taps otherwise. Subsidized loans gather no interest before payments (true for loans after July 1 2014; a few loans from 2012 to 2014 did pay grace-period interest, not modeled). (d) **Refinance**: the rate and the years are only the person's taps (4% to 8%, or typed), no default, no lender. "What you give up" names PSLF (with the dollars on their numbers), the payment set by income, the federal pauses and the death and disability discharge, and that it cannot be undone (CFPB). The private loan is a level fixed payment; a variable rate is not modeled. (e) **Household size**: a new field, Household size for student loans (on About you too). Blank means you, a spouse and the people you support, as before. (f) **One engine?** The game's student loan screen still has its own copy of the rules (`SL_REF` in the game, from before this run; the 2026 poverty line and the plan rules are typed twice). Parts 2 to 4 of this run made a build tool that copies the web math into the game. Do you want the same for student loans? I did not do it here, to keep the change small. The stepladder is on the web page only. (g) **Not verified by a person**: the screens were checked in Chromium at 390 and 1280 by me, with made-up numbers.

617. **RUN Not built, groups 1 to 5, Part 7: Hard moments. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **Sources.** Every phone number and every rule is in `data/sources/hard-2026.json` with the organization's own page and "checked Nov 25 2026". They were found by a web search limited to each organization's own site; the pages themselves do not open from the build machine (thehotline.org and opdv.ny.gov were refused), so please open each once: National Domestic Violence Hotline 1-800-799-7233, text START to 88788 (thehotline.org); NY State Domestic and Sexual Violence Hotline 1-800-942-6906, text 844-997-2121 (opdv.ny.gov); 988 (988lifeline.org); FEMA 1-800-621-3362 (DisasterAssistance.gov); Red Cross 1-800-733-2767; 211; HUD housing counselors 1-800-569-4287; NYC Tenant Helpline via 311 (Right to Counsel); No Surprises Help Desk 1-800-985-3059 (cms.gov); CFPB 1-855-411-2372; Social Security 1-800-772-1213 ($255 one-time payment, survivor benefits from 60, or 50 with a disability, 71.5% to 99%); LSC legal aid; AnnualCreditReport.com (weekly, FTC); IRS 501(r) (financial assistance policy, 120 days before extraordinary collection); the $400 good faith estimate dispute (cms.gov); FTC on co-signing, a relative's debts and telling one credit bureau about a death; IRS on inheritance (not income; the date-of-death value as the starting point). **Not sourced, so not in:** a Spanish line for the NY hotline (one older page lists 1-800-942-6908; I could not confirm it), legal-aid and housing help outside New York by state, and any cost (a divorce, a funeral, a lawyer): the tools ask for the person's own numbers and show none of their own. (b) **The quick exit.** I followed the GOV.UK Design System's "Exit this page" (a button always top right, and Shift pressed three times within 5 seconds); it blanks the page at once and goes to https://www.weather.gov/ with `location.replace`, so Back skips the page. It is on all eight pages (someone leaving an abusive partner may be on the untangling page too). The safety page says what it cannot hide: the visit stays in the browser's history unless a private window is used, and the Hotline's own line that history can never be fully erased. Is weather.gov the neutral site you want? Should the safety page carry a plainer name than safety.html ("A money plan, kept private" is its title)? (c) **Nothing kept by default.** The pages write nothing (not the address, not the device, not the account) until Keep this is on; off again takes the kept copy away. The hub's Hard moments links carry no plan in the address, so the page opens clean (the grief page then asks for spending instead of reading it from the plan). Kept, a tool is in `settings.hard` (web) or `c.x.hard` (game): a coach sees it only with Let my coach see this, a partner's view and anything shared never. In the game the database still holds a kept tool for a player who shares the game with a coach (the screen hides it, like the calm letter); a separate private table would need a database change. (d) **Scripts.** The medical, lending and other words are mine, written to copy, never to pressure. Part 1's creditor scripts wait behind `RC_SCRIPTS_REVIEWED`; these do not. Please read them (`data/sources/hard-2026.json`, tools.medical.scripts and tools.sayno.scripts); if you want them held for review the same way, say so. The lending page reuses the money sins' pause line, and grief and widowhood the 72-hour rule, so there is one set of those words. (e) **Untangling** is a list for talking it through, not a legal split (said on the page): split and sold things count at your share (half unless tapped), a debt in both names stays "owed by both" until it is paid off by a sale. (f) **Life Transitions:** the breakup ladder of run 1 Part 15 should link here (PROGRESS has the names). On the web the existing Life Transitions divorce template is not linked yet; the game links Hard moments under Break in case of emergency. (g) **Run 2 Part 6 (another lane, the same day) also builds a Leave page and a Keep switch for its LGBTQ+ tools.** The two should become one helper when both are on main; I did not touch that lane. (h) Not walked by a real person; checked in Chromium at 390 and 1280 by me with made-up numbers.

The card is the loudest script, with a percent for each of the four. Right framing? It is the one quiz over 15 taps; the test allows it by a flag. Too long? 620. **RUN Not built, groups 1 to 5, Part 9: Ratios on Key numbers. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **Where.** Web app: Report › Overview, a closed section Ratios with seven closed groups (Cash and cover, Debt and credit, Home, Money in and out, Earning, Wealth and later, How it feels). Game: a card Ratios at the end of the sheet Key numbers. Is the web place right, or do you want it on Home? (b) **Definitions I picked** (each is one line under Show the math): current ratio = cash ÷ short-term debt (cards, buy now pay later, payday, medical, personal and family loans); self-insurance threshold = cash − your emergency fund target (the Rule of 5 on the web, the full cushion in the game); solvency = net worth ÷ everything owned; total comp to base = (base + bonus + the job's match) ÷ base (employer-paid benefits are not in either app); income replacement = spending after work ÷ pay before taxes; human capital = pay before taxes until the stop age, brought to today at the plan's return, ÷ cash and investments; tax efficiency = the share invested in tax-advantaged accounts (retirement plans, IRAs, HSA, 529); longevity cover = (invested ÷ spending after work) ÷ (plan-to age − stop age), with no growth; windfall allocation = (cushion + debt + invested) ÷ (the windfall − tax on it). Different definitions wanted? (c) **Rules of thumb**: only two, credit mix (myFICO: about 10% of the score, not necessary to have one of each) and LTV (CFPB: ask to drop PMI at 80% of the original value, it ends at 78%), both read through a web search on Nov 26 2026 (the pages themselves do not open from here). Everything else says "No target here". I did not add the often-quoted 70% to 80% income replacement because I could not date one official source. (d) **Left out, logged on screen under What isn't here yet:** tax drag (neither app has what the taxable investments paid out); in the game the healthcare reserve (no out-of-pocket max) and the four home ratios (no home value); in the web app the windfall line (the steps live in the game). (e) **Financial confidence**: on the web it is the same Confidence rating Home asks for each session (tapping it on Ratios sets this session's answer), next to the scorecard's areas rated Strong or OK; in the game a new tap rating (`c.x.conf`), next to the key numbers in a good place. The words are kind and say which side is ahead, never a grade. OK? (f) **Real wage growth** reads the web app's income growth from Lifestyle creep (the ledger, or the tapped guess) and the game's Income growth line (take-home a year ago), each against the dated CPI figure. (g) Not walked by a real person; checked in Chromium at 390 and 1280 by me with the demo client and made-up numbers.

621. **RUN Not built, groups 1 to 5, Part 6: Queer-specific tools. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **Sources, all found by web search on Nov 25 2026 on each office's or organization's own site** (the build machine cannot open nycourts.gov, dmv.ny.gov and the rest, so I read the search results, not the pages; each list on the pages says "Checked on Nov 25 2026"): NY courts (NYC Civil Court $65; Supreme and County Court $210; papers sealed since Sept 23 2024), the Gender Recognition Act (S.4402-B, 2021: no newspaper notice), NY State Health (birth certificate gender change $0, one free copy, $30 each more), NY DMV ($12.50 to amend a license, $5 an ID; an older form ID-44 says $17.50, so please check once), Social Security (a name change card is free; since EM-25014 in 2025 it does not change the sex field), the State Department (DS-5504 $0 within a year, DS-82 $130, DS-11 $130 + $35; only M or F matching sex at birth under Executive Order 14168; the Supreme Court's Nov 6 2025 stay in Orr v. Trump; quoted as the page says it, no side taken), NY DFS (transition care and IVF rules, confidential claim letters), HRC CEI 2026 (the plan check items), IRS (2026 adoption credit $17,670, $5,120 refundable, phase-out $265,080 to $305,080; Form 8839 instructions and Rev. Proc. 2025-32; domestic partner cover as taxable pay), HealthCare.gov (a parent's plan to 26), Federal Student Aid (FAFSA unusual circumstances), nycourts.gov (who inherits with no will), NY State Health (free health care proxy form), HHS (HIPAA), Medicare (custodial care), SAGE, Point of Pride, Jim Collins Foundation, Trans Lifeline. Phone numbers only from the organization's own page: Trans Lifeline (877) 565-8860, The Trevor Project 1-866-488-7386 and text START to 678678, 988. (b) **Not sourced, so the person types it:** every care cost (surgery, hormones, IVF, surrogacy, adoption agency, care later in life), the fee for certified copies of a court order (each county clerk), the NYC birth certificate (its own pages say $0 and $40), and every state outside New York. Nothing on any page is a typical or guessed cost; cost boxes have no amounts to tap. (c) **Left out on purpose:** the SAGE elder hotline (its own notice says it closed in 2023; old pages still show the number), the 988 "press 3" option (ended July 17 2025, SAMHSA; the page says so and points to The Trevor Project), the Eldercare Locator number (I found it only on SAGE's page, not on the Locator's own). (d) **Privacy, my choices:** the ten pages use the same private mode and quick exit as the hard moments (Part 7), now one shared file (`js/solo/keepthis.js`): nothing is written to the address, the device or the server until Keep this is turned on; kept, a coach sees a page only if let in, and a partner's view and anything shared never do. Leave this page (or Shift three times) replaces the page with weather.gov. On top: the tab title is "Money tools" on every page, the file names are plain (carecost, idpapers, twosides, package, ownway...), and no-referrer. **Is "LGBTQ+ money tools, private" the shelf name you want on one.html?** (e) **The game: not ported, by choice.** A game profile is kept and the coach view reads it; putting these answers there needs a coach-hidden area first. The What's built row says so. **Do you want them in the game, kept away from the coach view?** (f) **Chosen-family estate plan:** run 1 Part 11 has not run, so I built the summary (`js/kin.js`, kin.html) so that Part 11 adds sections to `KIN_SECTIONS`; it writes a plain summary for a lawyer and says it is not a legal document. (g) **The closet calc** (twosides) shows two totals and how far apart they are, and nothing else: no "worth it", no advice. **Is that the tone you want?** (h) A lawyer or an LGBTQ+ legal group reading the NY steps once before launch would be good: the rules moved a lot in 2025.

622. **UX pass, phase 5 (dashboard, values, goals) and the test walk. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **The dashboard is My Money**, not Home. Home keeps its own row of four tiles, and for a player whose picture is not complete yet those are the four numbered tiles (Do this, Free to spend, Next steps, Your picture), which all open. Do you want Home in the same closed-section look? (c) **"Is it safe to spend this?"** waits until there is a goal (or "None of these, for now") and the home and bills lines are put in by you. Is that the right bar for "bills are known"? (d) **More / Less in values** is a tenth of the line, to the $10. (e) **Drag** works with a mouse on desktop; on a phone the ↑ and ↓ buttons do the same. (f) **Goals below the top 3** go to Someday and keep the era they had, and get it back when they move up. (g) **A card paid in full** is kept as a credit card row with $0 owed (from phase 4); it does not count in what you owe.

623. **RUN Not built, groups 1 to 5, Part 10a: The game (first half), Quest mode. What I chose, and what waits for you.** (a) **SLAM's own rules (please confirm or change):** the cushion counts full at 6 months and steady at 3; the margin full at 50% kept and thin under 10%; Rhythm full at 12 check-ins in a row; capacity is 1 new step under a 5% margin, 2 under 15%, 3 above, at most 1 with under a month of cushion, one less with a full plate; "about the middle" is within 10% of the median; a missing protection counts half in the order of the weak spots, so a cushion under half its mark comes first; the judge keeps the last 60 taps. All in `QS_RULES` (js/math/quest.js). (b) **The six stats are new and take their numbers from the plan** (Margin, Reach, Lightness, Coast, Clarity, Rhythm). The old Stats on Me (Earning, Saving, Flexibility, Know-how, Habits, People, 8 to 20) are untouched; two sets of stats now exist. Should Quest mode's six replace the old six on Me, or stay a separate quest view? (c) **Graduation privacy:** the money read joins the story only after the player taps Show my read (off by default, kept with the profile). The finish line is the player's tap among the journey's stages (Debt free, $100k, Coast, FI), FI by default. The story's dates come from the saved snapshots at today's Coast and FI numbers, and Debt free reads all debt in a snapshot (snapshots keep no costly-debt split). (d) **Opponents** are original (no names from any show or game): The Shiny Upgrade, The Group Chat Trip, The Lifestyle Lift, The Rescue Request, The Hot Tip, The Flash Sale, The Good Cause, The Shortcut Course. Their prices are tap choices, never a cost we claim for you. Beaten = the choice fits the three values from Is my money going where I want?; a win stays in the collection even if a later round goes the other way. Do you want more opponents, or ones for couples? (e) **The judge** holds five calls now: era, strongest stat, capacity, the money read and where you rank. Only the capacity and the money read change on a challenge (era, stat and rank follow the numbers, and say so). Other places that make calls about a person (the element, the quiz results, the identity on the character sheet) are not wired into it yet. (f) **Ask the game** is tap-only: five questions and eight library topics. No free text and no AI, as asked. (g) **Where you rank** uses the Federal Reserve's SCF 2025 net worth by age (Table 2) and the all-households percentiles (Table A.2) already in data/priors-2026.json; no percentile for your age is invented (the age band gives only the median). (h) **Nothing was added to Home** (another lane is rebuilding it). `qgHomeLine(c)` returns the first move of How this plan fails for a later Home tile. (i) Not walked by a real person; checked in Chromium at 390 and 1280 (each of the nine parts: no sideways scroll, no undefined or NaN, no console error).

624. **RUN Results-first Home, Items 1 and 2: What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **One reveal.** "Your first plan" is now "Here's where you stand": the lead number, the five cards, then Start my first win. The route (destination, stops, what the date depends on) is inside it behind "Your route, stop by stop". The front door ends on the same reveal, with "Keep this game: make a free account" under the cards; the answer to "Am I okay?" is in the Menu only now. The two taps after the first plan (Does this feel accurate?, What to improve most) and the element line left the end of setup; they stay on Me > Strengths and your element. OK? (b) **The lead number.** Below zero: the debt-free date at today's payments (no extra a month). A FIRE player, by their answers: "I put money in every month" on the investing question, or the freedom date switched on. Everyone else: the next stop of the route. Do you want another signal for FIRE/SPARKS (a question, the Money Chart)? Should the debt-free date use the money left each month instead of today's payments? (c) **The five cards.** Debt-to-income: target 36% (28/36), the time is the payoff run at today's payments until home plus the payments still open fit 36%; with no payment answers it uses each debt's typical payment and a 20% tax share, greyed as typical. Months of cushion: target your age ÷ 5 months (the game's rule), the time from the route. Savings rate: target 20%, then the Money Guy mark, then 50%; it has no time "at today's pace" (it does not move by itself), so the card says the gap a month instead. Net worth: below zero the target is $0; above it the next Set for Life stage ($25,000, $100,000). FI progress: the percent and the FI age and date from the game's own freeAt. (d) **Your next win** is the best move of the Moves library that fits your answers, an easy one first (effort 1 or 2); below zero, a move that frees cash for the debts comes first. A step that must come first (payday, a crossroads, a schedule due today, a heavy day, the gentle start) takes its place. The match shows "Check the fit" until its two fit taps are done. Is this the move you want first, or should the FOO step lead? (e) **By the end of this month**: dotted is today's net worth plus each day's money in less money out from the money calendar (debt payments count as moved, not spent; the month's interest is left out); solid adds the next win's money a month for the days left. Is that what you meant by "planned moves"? (f) **Moved off Home** (all still one tap away): the four boxes (Do this, Free to spend, Next steps, Your picture), the stat row (Free to spend, Net worth, Next goal, Free of costly debt), the next dollar line (now under Why this one on Your next win), Today's plan, and every card and row under them, behind See all of Home. Free to spend is still the top button Safe to spend? and on My Money; the money calendar is on Next Steps and in the Menu (My money > Money calendar). Only "Keep this game" and "Recalculating" rows stay on the folded Home. (g) Not walked by a real person; checked in Chromium at 390, 1280 and 1440 with three made-up players (see the report).
    **ANSWERED by Eli, Nov 26 2026 ((d), the move or the FOO step first). Built the same day:** "should be both what your goal is and then how to get there faster." Your next win now shows "Your goal now" (the step you are on: the debt-free step with its date below zero, else the FOO step with the next-dollar line and its amount to go; tap it to see the plan or the order) and under it "Get there faster" (the one move, its money a month, the days closer, what it unlocks, the one pulsing button). A FIRE type is never shown as the goal.

(i) Growth on the 10 years is a tap (0%, 4%, the plan's 7%); 0% is the start. Not legal advice: each screen says to bring it to a lawyer.

Nothing goes through an account, so no new table. A live version between two accounts would need the guild tables (not applied) or a new one: do you want that? (c) **Pass the phone**: the answers stay hidden until everyone has answered every question, or until someone taps "Show what we have so far". (d) **FI together** is in the game only: each person writes their FI number (taps from $500k to $3M, or typed, or the game's own: 25 times a year of spending) and date (year and month steps, or the game's own date). The gap is the largest number less the smallest and the latest date less the earliest. The web app's pod FI number (M9Y) is not shown next to it: do you want FI together in the web app too? (e) **A shared home** counts everything each person paid toward the home (at the start, plus each month x the months), and their part of the equity is that share; equal parts sit next to it, and neither is marked best. Monthly payments include interest, which builds no equity: should only the principal count? It does not split a rise or fall in the home's value differently from the rest. (f) **When only two can marry**: federal income tax only, on the game's 2026 tables (the retirement engine; the web's and the second engine's own M10 agree in the audit), everyone else single. Not in it: head of household, children, the state (New York), payroll tax, benefits. Each pair is worked out by Part 11's Married or partners? (`esCompare`, `js/math/estate.js`), not a second calculation, and the card links to that tab (health cover as taxable pay and the Social Security top-up are there, for two people). The web app has no polycule card: `married.html` compares two people. (g) **Share by email**: in the game, the picks are kept per person (G.hhx.mail); in the web app (One-pager › Share by email), the picks last while the box is open and are not saved, so a draft never goes out with old picks by surprise. Web defaults: every number and the next moves; the to-dos and the notes only for you. Should the picks be saved? (h) **Not verified**: a real mail app opening the draft on a phone, and two real phones trading codes; the game's Same question, FI together and shared home are not in the web app.

(g) **Roth or traditional**: the marginal income tax rate now (federal + state + city: web from your pay in the plan, game from the tax view) next to the same engine on an estimated income in retirement (web: what you spend now a year, or a tap; game: what you would spend at freedom less the Social Security estimate, or a tap) and where you might live (same place or a state with no income tax). 635. **RUN Finish everything partly built, Part 14: Taxes and self-employed. What I chose, for your yes or no, and what I could not source.** (a) **Tax command center** is the old page `setaside.html`, now titled "Tax command center: what to set aside, and by when" (the shelf on one.html says "Tax command center"). It keeps the set-aside share and adds: paid in estimates so far (counted with what is set aside toward what is owed so far), last year's total tax and whether last year's AGI was over $150,000 (Not sure uses the 110% share, the safe side), the safe harbor, a quarter at each date, what is owed by the next date and what would leave nothing owed in April. **Safe harbor rules** (`tax.safeHarbor` in the reference data): the smaller of 90% of this year's tax and 100% of last year's (110% over $150,000 AGI, $75,000 married filing separately), last year's return covering 12 months; none needed with less than $1,000 due after withholding, or with no tax last year over a 12-month year. Source: IRS 2026 Form 1040-ES, the IRS Estimated tax FAQ and Publication 505 (2026, dated Mar 31 2026), read through web search Nov 26 2026: irs.gov itself does not open from here. Please confirm the numbers against the form. (b) **What counts as this year's tax** for the safe harbor: federal only (a job's income tax, the work's income tax, its self-employment tax); state tax has its own rules and is not in it, and the page says so. A job's withholding is taken as the job's own income tax unless typed (the page had that rule already). Married filing separately has no tap in SLAM yet, so the $75,000 line is in the data but unused. The annualized installment method (Form 2210, for lumpy income) is not built. (c) **The game** has no tax engine, so its safe harbor reads this year's tax as a Guess: the player's own set-aside share on a year of the untaxed money of now, plus a tap for a job's withholding; last year's tax and the AGI line are taps. (d) **Pricing your power**: one optional tap "What a gig or app pays an hour, after its cut" on `freelance.html` and the game's My floor, shown next to the floor with the paid hours a month each needs. It compares an hour to an hour; the gig's own costs (miles, gear) are not taken off its pay, and the page says so. (e) **Platform P&L** is on `gigapps.html` (web only): each app's fee (typed), how often it pays out and the days to the bank, and the money that can still be on its way (at most the last cycle plus hold days of the stretch). Timing comes from the person's taps first, else from the platform's own help page, which I could read (through search, Nov 26 2026) only for **Uber** (help.uber.com: the week closes Monday, as early as Tuesday, 1 to 5 business days at the bank: I used 1 day), **DoorDash** (help.doordash.com: weekly, usually 2 to 3 days: 3 days) and **Upwork** (support.upwork.com: hourly, invoiced Monday, available the following Wednesday: 10 days; fixed-price a 5-day hold). **Not sourced from their own pages, so they wait for taps:** OnlyFans, Fansly, Patreon, Etsy; their older fee and hold rows (batch 27) are untouched and still feed the Income screen. Fees by platform from the platforms' own pages: none confirmed in this part. It is **not joined to the Tips and shifts P&L** (shift P&L, `js/math/shiftpnl.js`): that screen works from the plan's income rows, this page from the person's taps for one stretch. Should a Platform income row on Income show its payout window and money on its way too? The game has no apps list, so nothing there. (f) **Social Security replacement** is one function, `raSsReplace` (the estimate a year over pay before taxes a year), on the Lifetime plan's Social Security choice (each claim age shows "Replaces about N% of your pay today"), Report › Overview › Ratios (Wealth and later), the game's Retirement odds (a card "Social Security next to your pay") and the game's Ratios. The web reads its own estimate (benefitOf, the same as Key numbers); **the game reads its own older estimate** (`ssEst` in core/engine.js, from take-home over 0.8), so the two apps can still show different benefits for the same person: should the game move onto the web's benefitOf? (e) **Make your numbers real** asks the rows in the order of what they could move (not the list order), says "Not knowing this could move your money a year from now by about $X", and offers "Stop here, keep the rest as guesses" (an outlined button) before a row that moves little. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **The goal's words.** Below zero the goal reads "Be debt-free" with the date at today's payments, and a tap opens the Debt plan. Everyone else sees the name of their FOO step (for example "Prepay retirement") with the next-dollar line under it, and a tap opens Which account next (the order). The FOO step has an amount to go or room a month, not a date: the game has no date for each FOO step yet, so none is shown. Do you want a date there (it would be new math)? (b) **Other years or another return on the calculator.** The $30k / $90k rule is said for 30 years at 7%, so at those taps the headline is the rule, scaled by the amount (for $250: about $75k and about $230k). At any other tap (10, 20 or 40 years; 6%, 8% or 12%) the headline is the worked figure, rounded to two figures and marked "Rounded", because the round rule would not be true there (10 years: about $12k and about $17k, and the line says the two stay close). OK, or should the headline always be the rule? (c) **Round and exact are 20% apart at the default**, on purpose as you said: the headline says about $30k and about $90k, Show the math says $36,000 and $116,945, and one row says "The round rule: round on purpose, easy to remember". (d) **Days on the FI date.** In the game the card shows the days closer to FI for the amount cut (the Strategy Engine's own run). On the web page the row shows only when the page was opened from a plan with an FI date (the link from one.html carries it); it uses the plan's own return, not the tap. (e) **The game's card has taps only** ($25, $50, $100, $250, $500), no typed amount, like every Quick math card; the web page takes a typed amount. (f) **What's new** names no dollar amount (the rule of that list), so it says "about a third". (g) Not walked by a real person; checked in Chromium at 390 and 1280 with the three made-up players.

What changed: "What you have invested can grow to your FI number by the age you set, with nothing more added. From here, saving is a choice about how soon, not a race." [web: coast.html; game: Menu action paths]; (2) "Look at lower-paid work you like more": Your pay only has to cover today's bills, not tomorrow's. [web: #/plan/goals; game: Menu action cdn] **Lean** card "You reached Lean FI". What changed: "Your investments can pay for your needs for good: food, housing and transportation. [web: #/plan/goals; game: Menu action enough]; (2) "Pay for help at home": Cleaning or childcare buys back hours. See which tasks to hand off.
