645. **ANSWERED in part (Eli, Nov 27 2026): (e) growth is 7% for the whole chart and 4% is only what is taken out, so the pile ends higher, as a cushion: built. The KFF $703 a month health default: approved. Social Security at 100% from 67 on the chart, counted only after a tap: approved. The landing's second line: approved. (b) a tapped range stays a Guess (the coordinator's call). (c) the chip ranges: no change for now. Still open: (a) the two costs outside the number at the start, to be asked again in plain words.** **RUN A, Steps 0 to 3: What it is / What it could be. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **The two costs start outside the number.** Health insurance before 65 and tax on traditional withdrawals are on the chart from the start, as their own labeled parts. They enter "What it could be" only when the player taps "Count this in the number", so the two numbers start equal as you asked. Should they be counted from the start instead (then the second number starts higher than the first)? (b) **A tapped range is a Guess.** The four answers are range chips; the game counts the middle of the range and marks it Guess (setup's own range chips are marked Close). A typed amount is Exact. Yes? (c) **The ranges.** Age in 5-year chips; spending a month from "Under $2k" to "$10k or more"; invested from "Nothing yet" to "$1M or more"; saved a month from "Nothing right now" to "$4k or more". Edit freely. (d) **Until the accounts are listed, the invested amount counts as one traditional 401(k)**, the same way the game already treats a single total. So tax on withdrawals shows for everyone at first. (e) **Growth.** 7% a year while working and 4% a year after prices once drawing, the two rates the game already has; with 4% the 25 times number lasts for good, which is why the two numbers agree at the start. (f) **Tax.** Only the federal tax on the traditional money of that year. No state tax, no tax on Social Security, no 10% early charge before 59 and a half, no required withdrawals. Is that enough for a first chart? (g) **Health insurance.** The typical price is the KFF benchmark silver plan for a 40-year-old (plan year 2027, $703 a month in a state the table does not have, two adults for a married player), before any help with the price, greyed as typical. The player taps a range or types their own. It runs from the year work stops until 65. (h) **Social Security on the chart** is the game's own estimate at 100% from 67 (a Guess); the number counts it only after "Count this". The next step (4b) adds the presets. (i) **Switching a layer off** changes the chart and the gap. It changes the top number only when that layer was counted. (j) **The landing's second line** changed from 'The first question is yours: "Am I okay?"' to "First: four taps, then your freedom number." Your main line stays. "Am I okay?" is still the name of the rest of setup and of its Menu row. (k) **The rest of setup asks the age page again** only as a short "About you" page with the age filled in; the accounts and amounts of the four answers are kept, and a later answer replaces them. (l) Not walked by a real person. Checked in Chromium at 390 and 1280 with three made-up players.

646. **RUN A, Step 4 and the finish: the two strategy steps. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **The spending-shape default, for your approval.** Spending stays at 100% of today's to age 75, then 85% from 75 and 75% from 85. This is the default the web app already had (`retirementPhases` in `data/reference-2026.json`), so both apps use one. Source: BLS Consumer Expenditure Survey 2024, households 75 and over spent $55,834 a year against $65,354 at 65 to 74 (85.4%; FRED series CXUTOTALEXPLB0409M and CXUTOTALEXPLB0408M, read Nov 6 2026 and again through a web search on Nov 27 2026; the $65,354 is on the FRED page, the $55,834 was confirmed through a news article that quotes the survey, because bls.gov tables did not come up). The names go-go, slow-go, no-go are Michael Stein's. **The 75% from 85 is a convention, not a published figure**: the survey has no row for 85 and over. Approve it, give another number, or say "stop at 85%". (b) **A second preset, "Half as much easing"** (92.5% from 75, 87.5% from 85): the middle between the typical shape and no change, as the careful choice. It is worked out from the default, not from a source. Keep it? (c) **Social Security presets.** All of it; "Count part of it: 83%" (the 2026 Trustees Report: what can still be paid from 2034 with no change in the law; already in the reference data); "Count part of it: 75%" (the careful choice setup already offers); "Not counting on it". Are two "part" chips one too many? (d) **One home for the share.** The preset writes the game's own Social Security share (`c.x.ss`), the one setup and the odds use. So "Not counting on it" or "Not for me" on this screen also takes Social Security out of the rest of the game's numbers, until the player counts it again (setup asks the same question later). Yes? (e) **Always a Guess for now.** You asked for Guess "unless the player has entered their own figure". The game has no box for a player's own Social Security figure today; the estimate always comes from pay. So the screen always says Guess and points to the statement at ssa.gov. A box for the player's own monthly figure is a new field that the whole engine would read: build it in Run B? (f) **"Already doing it" counts it too.** It is true of the player, so the number includes it; the receipt says "Already yours, and counted". Or should it leave the number alone? (g) **The receipt compares with and without this one step** (not with the screen before), so changing a preset never shows a confusing minus. (h) **A gap can show after counting.** With the shape and Social Security counted, the number gets lean enough that health insurance before 65 and tax (on the chart, not in the number, item 645a) leave late years "Not covered yet". The screen now says that plainly and offers "Count health insurance before 65" and "Count tax on traditional withdrawals" right there; counting them raises the number and closes the gap. If you would rather count the two costs from the start, say so (645a). (i) **Switching a layer off can move the age.** A steady income that is counted and then switched off is no longer counted, so the age moves later (work goes on longer) and the chart can show fewer open years than before. The rule "a layer that is off never raises coverage" is tested at the same stop age. (j) **Layer colors.** A color theme of the game has one hue, so Work, Investments and Social Security now differ by how light they are (Social Security is the palest; it was green). Fine?

647. **BATCH B, Phase 1: top-ups to Run A. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **What "the rest of your life costs" counts.** It is what you spend in a year today, for every year from your age now to 95, in today's dollars: the same spending each year, no growth, no health insurance before 65, no tax. For a 27-year-old spending $3,500 a month that is 69 years and about $2.9M. It is shown rounded to the thousand and marked Guess, Close or Exact like the FI number. It does not change when you count a way (it sits on "What it is", the floor). Right, or should it stop at another age, or follow the spending shape once that is counted? (b) **The line under it.** For anyone under about 70 the total is more than the FI number, so the screen adds "Your FI number is smaller: what is invested keeps growing while you spend from it." Keep that line? (c) **"Count part of it" is 83%**, the share the 2026 Trustees Report says can still be paid (already in the reference data). The 75% chip is gone as a separate chip. A player who chose 75% in setup keeps 75% as their part, so setup and this screen never disagree. Fine, or should the part always be 83%? (d) **The order of the three chips** is yours (Not counting on it, Count part of it, Count all of it). Before any tap, the drop shown is for all of it, as in Run A. Fine, or should the drop start at the part? (e) **The last screen until the later phases land.** "More ways to shrink it are coming" is gone. The card "What you counted" now lists every way in the flow (two today) and each later phase adds its own row. The buttons are still Finish setup, Next Steps and Home; Phase 4 replaces them with your three.

648. **BATCH B, Phase 2a: back-end taxes. What I chose, for your yes or no, and the tax figures for your approval.** (a) **What "Count this" does.** The 25 times rule counts no tax. So this way cannot lower a number that has no tax in it. I built it like this: the screen shows what the tax adds the usual way (taxable first, then traditional, then Roth), what it adds with the plan, and "The drop for you" is the plan against the usual order. "Count this" puts the tax into your number the lower way, so What it could be goes up from the no-tax number and the top of the screen says "Above What it is, because you counted tax on traditional withdrawals". Example (made-up player, 27, $40K traditional, $20K Roth, $15K taxable): the usual order adds $41,000, the plan adds $19,000, the drop is $23,000 after rounding. Is that the right shape, or should the tax be counted for everyone from the start so that this way only ever lowers the number? (This is the same open question as 645(a).) (b) **A new player sees "No drop yet".** The four taps make one traditional plan at work. With one kind of account there is nothing to move tax between, so the plan saves nothing and the screen says so in plain words, with a button to the rest of setup. All four test personas land here. Do you want one more tap in the flow ("Where is your invested money? Mostly traditional, a mix, mostly Roth") so the way can show a drop for a new player? I did not add it: your prompt says two taps. (c) **The number with tax counted changed for a mix of accounts.** Before, the number taxed the same share of every year. Now it takes each year's tax from the same year-by-year run as the chart (the usual order), so the number and the chart agree. For money that is all traditional nothing changed. For a mix the number with tax counted is a little higher than before. Yes? (d) **State tax.** Your state's income tax is now counted on traditional money once you tap a state. It comes from the tax engine's state table (42 states and DC with an income tax; source Tax Foundation, State Individual Income Tax Rates 2026, engine data dated Apr 30 2026). It is the regular table for pay. Many states tax retirement income more lightly (New York, for one, leaves out part of pension and IRA income from 59 and a half; I did not confirm the amount here): that is not counted, so the state tax here runs high. Show the math says so. Keep it this way, or count federal tax only until the state rules for retirement income are built? I did not check the 42 state tables figure by figure. (e) **New York, head of household.** The game's tax engine uses the single table and the $8,000 deduction for a head of household in New York. `data/reference-2026.json` has its own head of household cutoffs and $11,200 (still marked not checked against the law). Single and joint agree in both. Which is right? Nothing was changed. (f) **The 0% capital gains zone.** The game does not know the gains in a taxable account (what was paid for the investments), so it counts no tax on them. The screen shows the room that is left under the 0% line in the years taxable investments are sold, and says the gains are not counted. Do you want one tap for it ("About how much of your taxable account is gain?") in a later phase? (g) **The filing status guess.** The flow now reads the tax view's guess until you tap: married is filing together, single with kids is head of household, else single. Before, single with kids counted as single here. Yes? (h) **Not counted, and said so on the screen:** tax on Social Security, required withdrawals from 73, the 10% charge before 59 and a half, the 5-year wait on converted money, the extra deduction at 65 (the engine uses your age today for every year), and changes of the law. (i) **Tax figures, tax year 2026, for your approval.** All federal. Standard deduction: $16,100 single, $32,200 married filing together, $24,150 head of household. Brackets, by taxable income, single: 10% to $12,400, 12% to $50,400, 22% to $105,700, 24% to $201,775, 32% to $256,225, 35% to $640,600, 37% above. Married filing together: 10% to $24,800, 12% to $100,800, 22% to $211,400, 24% to $403,550, 32% to $512,450, 35% to $768,700, 37% above. Head of household: 10% to $17,700, 12% to $67,450, 22% to $105,700, 24% to $201,750, 32% to $256,200, 35% to $640,600, 37% above. Long-term gains at 0% up to taxable income of $49,450 single, $98,900 married filing together, $66,200 head of household; at 15% up to $545,500, $613,700, $579,600. Source of all of them: IRS Rev. Proc. 2025-32. irs.gov does not open from the build machine, so they were read through web search on Nov 27 2026: the IRS news release (standard deduction), Kiplinger, NerdWallet, The Week, Experian and Current Federal Tax Developments. Two cutoffs did not show in any search result I could read and stand on the earlier entries of the file alone: $403,550 (joint, 24% to 32%) and $256,200 (head of household, 32% to 35%). The plan only uses the lines up to the top of the 22% bracket. They live in `data/reference-2026.json` (`tax`), and a test fails if the game's tax engine ever differs from that file.

649. **BATCH B, Phase 2: the rest of the stack (2b to 2f). What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **How the flexible rate is found.** Nobody types a rate. The game takes each real start year of market history (1871 on), stops work at the age of What it is, and runs the real markets after it to 95, twice: with the fixed 4% rule and with guardrails (cut 10% when the share you take is 20% above the start, raise 10% when 20% below, never under 70% of spending; the rule the game already had). The rate is the highest one, in steps of 0.1%, at which guardrails held in at least as many start years as the fixed 4% rule. For the 27-year-old of the walk that is 5.2%, so 19.2 times a year of spending in place of 25. Is "as safe as the 4% rule was in history" the right bar? (b) **The mix in that test** is the share in stocks the retirement odds already use for the player (90% for a 27-year-old). Should the test use one fixed mix, such as 60%, for everyone? (c) **The backup of flexible withdrawals** is "your needs fit inside the deepest cut" (needs at 70% of spending or less). A new four-tap player has the game's standard split (needs 75%), so the backup shows "Not in place yet" until the real spending is in. Right? (d) **Money that shows up later.** A pension starts at 65: the game has no question for its age. A home loan counts only as a payment that ends; the value of the home (selling, a smaller home) is not counted. Other payments that end which setup already knows (childcare) are counted by the same way. Do you want a tap for the pension age, and do you want home equity as its own way? (e) **A counted move before FI does not change the two numbers on top.** Months sooner for the moves comes from the engine those cards already use (the month-by-month goal run of Moves to try). The two numbers on top come from the flow's own function. They are two engines, and I was told not to work out months a second way, so the stack shows "The 2 moves you counted, together: 32 months sooner" beside the numbers and says why. Do you want counted moves to move the date on top? That needs one engine chosen for both. (f) **Which cards are in the accumulation stack.** The 13 moves of the Strategy Engine (job match, HSA, bank bonus, card welcome bonus, house hack, rent a room, raise, side income, cheaper city, car swap, 0% transfer, consolidation, 0% purchase card), plus the 5 levers and multipliers no move covers (trim a tenth of spending, fees and idle cash, card rewards, buying ahead, a tax-favored account). "Borrowing to invest" is left out. Right list? (g) **The stack screen.** The chart leads to one list of every way; a way opens by choice, or one tap in the list counts it; the first 3 moves show and "Show all" opens the rest. A new player reaches the last screen in 8 taps, 11 with three ways counted. (h) **The backups meter** counts the ways that shrink the number (each has its own backup). The moves before FI all have the same backup (your date at today's pace does not count on them), so they are not on the meter. (i) **"3 of 14 stacked"** counts from the ways that can apply to the player now, so the 14 changes when an answer opens a way. Right, or should it count from every way? (j) **Not mine, found on the way:** `game/CODE-MAP.md` lists one function with no caller and no reason (`stgCredit` in strat.js). It was so on main before this phase.

650. **BATCH B, Phase 3: the "Yes, but" card. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **Whose idea is it?** Your prompt says to credit the idea like the other famous ideas, and names no one. I did not guess. The row is in Famous money ideas ("Yes, but: a cut that pays for a keep") and its credit line reads "A well-known trade-off idea. The name of its source is to be added here". Who should it name? (b) **What a cut is.** There is no list of typical cuts with typical prices (a streaming plan, a gym). Each Yes is one of your own spending lines, cut by a tenth, a quarter or half (three chips). So no price is made up. A new player has the five lines of the four taps (Home, Food, Getting around, Bills and subscriptions, Fun and everything else). Is that right, or do you want named cuts with sourced typical prices as well? (c) **What a keep costs.** "Keep as it is" costs no months: it is already in your date, so the net is the price of the Yes. "A tenth more" or "A quarter more" costs months later. Is that the meaning of "keep or upgrade" you want? (d) **The card is a what-if.** It changes no spending line, and it does not move What it could be, the counter, or the months of the counted moves, though a real cut would shrink the number too (25 times the cut). The stack shows the net of the card on its own row. Should a card count in the reveal of phase 4, and should it move What it could be? (e) **Which engine.** Months come from the month-by-month goal run behind Moves to try (`stgImpact`), because it handles a monthly change of spending: more saved each month, and a smaller FI number. The net is one run with both halves, so it can differ by a month or so from the two prices subtracted. The screen says so in Show the math. Fine? (f) **Pairs with no ranked values.** Until you rank what matters (Is my money going where I want) and say what each line is for, the pairs go by size: the biggest line as the cut, the smallest as the keep. For a new player the first pair is therefore "Yes: Home. But: Bills and subscriptions", which reads oddly. The "i" says that the order is by size and is no opinion. Would you rather the first pair leave Home out, or ask the top three values as one tap on this screen? (g) **One card, not a stack of cards.** A player has one card at a time (any number of lines in each half) and can save it as an image again after each change. Enough, or do you want several kept cards? (h) **The image has no dollar amount**, like the other share cards: line names, the size in words, the months. Its last line is "A what-if on my own numbers. Education and tools, not financial advice." Fine? (i) **A repair you should know of.** The stack of phase 2d showed the error screen for a player with costly debt (a locked multiplier had no math lines). It is repaired and has a test now.

651. **BATCH B, Phase 4: reveal, share, Home, day 2. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **Two screens at the end, not one.** Your 4a asks for the chart with all its switches and your 4b for a last screen with three buttons only, so the reveal is its own screen (tap 8 from the landing) and the last screen comes after it (tap 9). Yes? (b) **No dollar amount on the shared card.** "Share my card" makes an image with the two ages ("FI at age 53", "FI at age 47"), the time between them, and the names of up to three things you counted. No dollars, the same as the Yes, but card. Do you want the two dollar numbers on it? (c) **Save my plan.** A visitor with no account gets the existing "Create your account" screen (email and password; passkeys stay off). A signed-in player sees one line that says the plan is already in the account. Yes? (d) **Where "Finish setup: five short rounds" lives now.** On the first tile of Home and on Next Steps, until it is done. It is no longer on the last screen. Yes? (e) **Top 3 moves on Next Steps.** They are the moves you counted first, then the ones with the most days sooner; a move you already do or left out is not one. Each is a card that opens its full card, and "Add to my steps" (one tap) puts its steps on your plan. Nothing is added to the plan without that tap. Should the three be added by themselves when you reach the reveal? (f) **Deeper features.** Spending eases and Social Security both open Retirement odds. The tax plan opens Your tax view. Flexible withdrawals opens Taking money out, Guardrails. Money that shows up later opens Make it exact (the game has no pension feature of its own). Health insurance opens Taking money out, Health cover. A move opens its full card, a lever opens Which lever matters most, a multiplier opens the multipliers board, the Yes, but card opens Is my money going where I want. Is any of these the wrong door? (g) **The moves and the Yes, but card are on their own line.** The difference between the two numbers is only what the one calculation of the flow gives. "The moves you counted: N months sooner" sits under it with an "i" that says why. Joining the two is new math (also asked at 649). (h) **Who sees the tile on Home.** Only a player who has been in the flow. A player from before, who never opened it, sees Home as it was. Should everyone get the tile? (i) **Day 2.** "Since last time" compares your numbers now with your numbers at the end of the last day you played, in the words of the game's receipts. "Nothing else pulses": on the Home of a player of the flow only the button of Your next win pulses, on every day, not only on day 2 (the dot on You are here is still for them). Yes? (j) **Two ways to the report tool on the last screen.** "Tell Eli what broke" is the third button, and the small Report button that every sheet has is still at the bottom. Take the small one off this screen?

652. **BATCH B, Phase 5: a calmer first week. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **"This flow" is a fourth button in the bar.** In the first week the bar reads Home, Next Steps, My Money, My number. "My number" opens What it is / What it could be where you left off. Yes, or should the flow be reached from Home only (three places in the bar)? (b) **+ Log, Safe to spend? and Where am I also wait in the menu.** Your prompt says "show only" the four, so the + Log button leaves the bar and the two buttons leave the top row for the week. All three are menu rows (Log money is under "Back in the bar after your first week"). Do you want + Log to stay in the bar during the first week? (c) **A player who never sees the reveal stays in the calmer bar.** "After 7 days or after the reveal, whichever is later" has no end for someone who never taps to the reveal. Nothing is lost (the menu has it all), but the bar never grows. Do you want a last day, such as day 30, when the tabs return anyway? (d) **A player who did not come through the flow** (an account made with the old setup) has no reveal to wait for: the tabs return on day 8. Yes? (e) **What "first day" is.** The day setup ended (the game already keeps it as the first line of its history), so no new date is stored. A game counts from its oldest person: adding a partner later does not start a new first week. Yes? (f) **Existing players.** A game older than 7 days sees no change in the bars, and neither does a saved game with no first day on file. One case to know: a game that started in the 7 days before this went live gets the calmer bar until its day 8 (and its reveal, if it came through the flow). Fine? (g) **Coach and admin accounts are never calmed**, also when they play as a player. Yes? (h) **The Tool library is for everyone, not only the first week.** It is one new group in the menu of every player: 83 pages in 10 groups, with the names and groups of the web app's own front page. Each opens in a new tab, and what is typed there does not come into the game. Left out: the web app's front page and its whole plan, the page for coaches, and the page of example numbers. Yes? (i) **ANSWERED (Eli, Nov 27 2026): "Lgbtq is an add on and should be treated the same as [the other two add-ons] in that they are not available to standard gamers." The group and its pages are out of the game for standard players (see 653d).** **One group in it was named "LGBTQ+ money tools, private"**, the name the web app's front page uses. Is that name fine in the game's menu, or do you want a quieter one? (j) **"New for you" stays on Home until "Got it" is tapped.** It is one line and does not pulse. Should it go away by itself after a few days? (k) **Home itself is not thinned.** Only the bars and the menu change. The rows under "See all of Home" are as before. Do you want Home shorter in the first week too? (l) **The top row keeps the person button and Report** in the first week.

653. **BATCH B, Phase 7: ready for real testers. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **Three tasks in place of five.** Test with a friend (admin menu) now has your three tasks, in your words: find out what your number could be; switch Social Security off and say what changed; get back Home and find your next move. The five older tasks are gone. Tester drives (the test a person runs alone, also the address with ?think=1) uses the same three; its three older tasks are gone too. Yes? (b) **What is recorded.** For each task: how many taps, the names of the screens in order (40 at most), the screen where the person stopped, the time, and what the coach taps (did it alone, needed a nudge, got stuck, where, why). No sound, no picture of the screen, no value of any field. The record is part of the saved game on the device (the last 20 tests). A saved game already goes to the player's account when they are signed in; nothing new is sent and there is no new table. "Copy these results" gives the text to paste. Fine? (c) **Finish setup comes back to the page you left.** The repair of the browser walk showed that a player who left "Finish setup: five short rounds" in the middle started again at page 1 and lost the answers of the round. Now the row "Finish setup" on Home and Next Steps opens the page they left, for 7 days. "Where am I" does not point back to it: its Continue card shows the next part of the journey. Should Where am I offer "Finish setup, at the page you left" first? (d) **The LGBTQ+ money tools in the game: excluded, not yet switchable.** After your words (652i) the group and its ten pages are out of the Tool library, out of Find a tool, and the What's new entry about them does not show in the game. The existing add-on switch could not be used without the database (an add-on is a row there with a sealed file), so the pages are held out by the tool that makes the list, with the reason "add-on: off for standard players". "Every tool on one page" (one.html) is held out of the library too, because it shows the group to every visitor. Not changed, by instruction: the web app. There, one.html shows the fold "LGBTQ+ money tools, private" to everyone, the web app's What's new shows the entry, and each of the single pages has a link to one.html, so a player who opens any page of the Tool library is one tap from the group. Hide them in the web app too? The What's built list (admin only) still has its row for them. (e) **Idea from Eli, not built.** A few optional questions about the player's household, so the game can recognise who an add-on fits and offer it as an option (LGBTQ+ households are one example; the same for the other add-ons). The answers are sensitive, so the coordinator will bring a design first. Nothing was built. (f) **Phase 6, the in-debt version, was skipped, not built**, under "skip and report if the run is already long". (g) **Not walked by a real person.** The three tasks were done by the test and in Chromium at 390 wide.

Please open it on your phone before you print. If it is not live by Nov 30: change LINK in `tools/qr_era.py` to the old address and run it again. (h) **The words on the printed page**: "Find your money era in 60 seconds", "Taps only. No typing. No account.", and your line "Built by Eli Saperstein, money coach. 250+ people coached." Change any? 655. **ONBOARD-1: worker 1 (tasks 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7). What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **The typical amount invested is not checked on the Fed's own page.** Under 35 $21,000, 35 to 44 $61,200, 45 to 54 $133,000, 55 to 64 $239,000, 65 to 74 $257,000: the median retirement account of families that have one, Survey of Consumer Finances 2025. (b) **Typical spending is a household's, not one person's.** It is the BLS average for all households of the age (25 to 34: about $5,310 a month), less payroll taxes and retirement contributions, the rule the web app already uses. The BLS has no one-person table by age. Keep it? (c) **Typical saving has no table by age.** I used the country's saving rate (BEA, 3.8% in September 2026) on the typical spending of the age: about $210 a month at 25 to 34. (g) **The first button for a signed-in player says "Keep my plan"** (they already have an account); for everyone else "Keep my plan: make a free account". Buy a home matches no move (the housing moves are for a home you already have). (k) **The new first screen costs one tap**: 10 taps from the landing to the last screen, 9 before. Counted again on both versions with `tools/game_clicks.cjs`: 6 taps to the first number (5 before), 44 taps to "Here's where you stand" (43 before). The counter's limit for the first number is now 6. (l) **The new address** could not be opened from the session. The file CNAME in the repository already names play.stresslessaboutmoney.com. Please open https://play.stresslessaboutmoney.com/game/ on your phone. (m) **The web app's address** in `docs/RELEASE.md` still says sapphirestoneage.github.io/Slam/. Change it to play.stresslessaboutmoney.com too?

The two numbers are a tile lower on Home. Is that right, or should the demo player still see the two numbers once? Your answers save to your account when you start your first move.", because nothing is saved until the game opens. The demo page tells a sign-in by two marks the game keeps on the device; it does not ask the server. Do you want the exact words "Saved to your account." there too? Keep the font, or go back to the device's own font? (h) **The first move stays the top card of Home until the player taps Start this move.** It does not time out. A move of the Moves library also puts its steps on Next Steps at that tap. Right? (i) **Home, the four tiles, in this order:** Free to spend (this month), Next goal, Net worth, Free of costly debt. One "Show the math" sits under the row, with a line for each number, and each tile has its own "i"; the full math of each number is one tap away on the tile. A button cannot hold a second button, so the tile's number is the button and the "i" is next to the label. Is one shared Show the math enough? (j) **Free to spend is the month everywhere on Home now** (the tile, the card lower on Home, the Safe to spend sheet), with the week as a smaller piece ("about $282 a week"). The Ask front door still shows Today, This week and This month side by side. (k) **"Free of costly debt" for a player nobody asked about debts** says "Now, none at 8% or more, Guess", greyed. After setup it is the month from the debt plan, or "No end yet" when a payment is missing. There is no "this month" number for debt, so nothing is made up. Would you rather it say "Not asked yet"? (l) **Next goal** says "None yet, add one on My Goals" when no goal fits. A goal picked on What you want counts once it has an amount and a place. (m) **Below the fold, in this order:** Your two numbers (with Finish setup), By the end of this month, You are here, Your numbers (FI date, savings rate, net worth, months of cushion, See all metrics). Net worth is on Home twice: the top tile and Your numbers. Both read the same number. Drop the lower card? (n) **Not asked twice.** Filled, with one tap to change: age, the amount invested (when there is one investment account), the cushion questions, the kinds of debt and the tax-friendly answer (these three only for a visitor of the demo, where they were asked). Spending opens with the share of your own quick answer in place of a stock number. Still asked: income (the opening never asked it; it only added spending and saving), each spending line, each debt's amount and rate. (o) **A filled page still shows, with its answers and Next.** The age page is passed over; the cushion and debts pages show what you said and take one tap. Pass over them too? (p) **The number animation:** a dollar number counts for 0.6 seconds and turns mint; a number in words (a date, a range) turns mint for a moment. With "reduce motion" on the device, nothing moves. (q) **The registry files in game/registry (fields.json, AUDIT.md)** still describe Home before this change. They were made by a tool that is not in the repository, so I did not edit them. (r) **Nothing needed server work.** No table, no SQL, no key.

659. **RUN 14, the usability pass on What it is / What it could be: worker 1 (items 1 to 7). What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **Forward is not on the result screen.** Your newer prompt asks for one button and a small Back there, so I left Forward off that one screen. Back from the result opens the reveal, and Forward on the reveal returns to the result. (b) **No "i" on the result screen,** for the same reason. Every other screen has them. (e) **"Most likely" for spending, invested and saving** is the range that holds the typical value for the age (the same values "I'm not sure" uses). The invested figures by age are still the ones read through a web search and not yet checked on the Fed's own page (question 655). (f) **The one line about the choice you tapped shows on the next screen** ("Your last tap. $3k to $4k a month: the game counts $3,500 a month, $42,000 a year."). A tap on an answer moves on at once, so the line cannot show on the same screen without a second tap for each question (4 more taps to the first number). Say so if you want the second tap. (g) **No jump to the top means a new screen opens where the last one stood.** After a long screen with its button at the bottom, the next screen opens partway down, at its own next button. That is what you asked for; it also means a person can miss the headline of the next screen. With the folds most screens are now short. Yes or no. (h) **What is folded behind one tap:** what the number means and the lifetime total (What it is); the chart in words, and the switches of the layers and costs (the chart and the reveal); what has to go right, the backup and the plan for your numbers (each way); the two lists of the Yes, but card (the pairs stay open); the list of what you counted (the reveal). Tell me if one of these has to stay open. (i) **The words "save".** "Save this card as an image" is now "Get this card as an image". I left two things that the game does by itself: the line "Saved to your account." on the result (your words of run 16) and the small green "Saved" mark at the top of the game. Say so if they should read "Kept". (j) **Two Backs.** The Back in the new row opens the step before in the flow, as the old Back buttons did. The Back at the top of the sheet opens the screen you came from. They can differ (a way opened from the list). Say so if you want one of them gone. (k) **How pale.** Each control that is not the next tap is at 80% (a pale range button at 70%). In the standard look the words keep a contrast of 9 to 1 and 7 to 1. I did not measure the other color themes. (l) **The older "i" buttons for single words** (FI number, how you file, and so on) keep their one longer text. The new ones have the two lines, and the longer text under "More about it" where one existed.

660. **RUN 14, the usability pass on What it is / What it could be: worker 2 (items 8 to 13). What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **A tap outside closes an open "i", not the opening itself.** A tap outside an "i" (or a word of the glossary) closes it and you are on the same screen again. A tap outside a screen of the opening does nothing: closing there would drop a new visitor's answers on a stray tap. Say so if you want a tap outside to close the opening too (the X at the top still does). (b) **"0 of" is gone by waiting, and by words.** Before the first count the stack shows one line, "Tap Count this on a row. Your count shows here." After it: "1 of 14 counted". When no backup is ready yet it reads "Backups ready: none yet. 1 to check". Is that the wording you want? (c) **The counter now says "counted", not "stacked"** ("3 of 14 counted"). The screen is still called The stack. Rename the screen too? (d) **The reveal has your top 3 moves now** (your item 9 lists them). They are the same three as on Next Steps. A tap opens the move inside the opening, and Back returns to the reveal. (e) **"What you counted" on the reveal is closed** under the difference (one tap opens it), so the first block stays short on a phone. The counter is inside it. (f) **The countdown counts taps on the main button**: "4 taps left, then your number" on the first question, "4 taps left to your result" on What it is, down to 1 on the reveal. The result has none. The side screens (a layer, a move) have none. What you want has none: its button already says "Next: four quick taps". (g) **The why of each question** is one new line under the help line, for example "Asking because you picked Retire early. Your age sets how many years your money can grow." With no pick it starts "Why we ask:". The questions themselves are as you wrote them. Please read the four lines. (h) **Undo is said, not a new button.** A counted way or move says "To undo, tap Not for me." The stack says "To undo one, tap Counted on its row." The Yes, but card has "Undo: clear the card" (it was "Start the card again"). (i) **One thing per step: I said it first and did not split.** Buttons that leave the opening (the full card of a move, Is my money going where I want, Finish setup on the tax way) have one line before them. "Start this move" on Home does two things (it puts the steps on Next Steps, then opens the move): the card now says so. Do you want that split into two buttons? (j) **Wording: not every line.** I reworded every "i", the hints, the stack, the reveal, the receipts and the buttons. I did not reword the long texts in Show the math, the glossary texts, and some lines inside the closed parts of the tax way, the flexible way and the Yes, but card. Do you want those done in a pass of their own? (k) **Buttons renamed**: the chart's "Next" is "Next: ways to shrink it"; a way's "Next" is "Next way", "Next: the Yes, but card" or "Next: the reveal"; "All the ways" is "See all the ways"; "See where this leaves you" is "Next: the reveal". (l) **"Off the target" is "off your number"** on every receipt, so the opening uses one word for it.

661. **RUN 15 (CC-1), part A, Reliability (items 1 to 6). What I chose, for your yes or no, and what only you can do.** (a) **Quiet Quitting FI has one engine.** The app has it (the amount invested today after which the yearly minimum alone reaches the FI number), and the new math checks prove it by growing the money year by year. The second engine (the Python one) does not have it yet, so that one check is marked "to do". Do you want it added to the second engine? (b) **"7/4/2" I read as: 7% return after inflation, 4% withdrawal rate, 2% inflation.** These are the defaults in the reference data, and the check holds them there. The game has the 7 and the 4; it works in today's dollars, so it has no inflation rate. Is that the 7/4/2 you mean? (c) **The backup file can be downloaded by anyone with a GitHub account**, because the repository is public and GitHub shows the files of a workflow run to every signed-in person. The file is encrypted, so the passphrase is the only lock. The backup refuses a passphrase under 20 characters. Is that acceptable, or do you want the backups sent to a private place instead (a private repository, or a storage bucket of your own)? (d) **The test build uses the real saved games.** `/game-next/` and `/game/` are on one website, so a browser gives both the same storage, and an account saves to the same database. The strip says so. Try a new build with a test account. A separate database for the test build would need a second Supabase project: do you want that? (e) **The public site is now a branch the publish script builds** (the files of main, plus the folder `game-next/`), not a pointer to a commit of main. The command and its last line are the same. A change made on `release` by hand is replaced by the next publish. (f) **The note "What's new" on Home**: a player who was here before an update sees one quiet line with three buttons (See what changed, Got it, Don't show again). A new player sees none until the next update. "Don't show again" is undone in Menu, What's new. Is one note on Home fine, or do you want it only in the Menu? (g) **Report a bug was already built** and the database already has its table (it holds reports today). I added nothing to the game for it: the Menu row is "Report a bug or idea", the categories are Bug, Idea and Confusing. Do you want more categories (for example "A number looks off", "Sign-in")? That needs one small database change. (h) **The privacy page says "no affiliates"** in these words: SLAM gets no money when you pick a card, a bank or a product. Tell me if a paid link is planned, because then that line must change first. (i) **The backup holds the accounts and the public tables only** (the schemas `auth` and `public`). The full restore of a lost project is written down but was not tried on a real Supabase project.

662. **RUN 15 (CC-1), part B, Faster (items 8 to 11). What I chose, for your yes or no, and what only you can do.** (a) **Time to the first number: the clock starts when the first screen shows, and stops while the page is out of view.** The seconds do not count time in another tab. The taps are the taps of the opening (Start is tap 1). A person who comes from Find your era or from the form before a call is not counted: that person never sees the screen What it is. Count them too? (b) **The measure is kept in two places.** In the saved game of an account (the admin page reads it there today, with no database change), and in a new table for visitors with no account, which waits for you to paste one file. Until then the admin page counts players with an account only, and says so. (c) **One line on the privacy page** says that the game keeps the seconds and taps of the first number, with no name. Change the words if you want. (d) **Questions that moved out of setup.** "Do you share your money with anyone, like a joint bank account?" (page Your people) and "How many roommates live with you?" (page Your home) are now one-tap quests on Next Steps. Until you answer, shared money counts as No one (a Guess) and the roommates count as one, as "Skip" did before. Setup never asked about subscription splits, so nothing moved: a player with roommates or a partner gets a quest that opens Sharing costs fairly. (e) **A single player gets no quest about shared money.** The game counts No one for them, and Change my answers still has the question. Ask single players too? (f) **"How many kids do you have?" stays in setup.** You named roommates, not kids, and the count of kids moves the tax side. Move it too? (g) **Starting points: which values each one fills.** Only three typical figures have a source: spending by age (BLS), invested by age (the Fed's survey, families that have a retirement account), and the saving rate of the country (BEA). Your age is always asked. I left a value empty where the figure does not describe the situation: Just laid off (saving), Living with parents (spending and saving), Student (invested and saving), Near FI (invested and saving). Card debt has no typical balance with a source, and New baby has no typical cost of a baby: setup asks those. Say if you want a figure for any of these, with its source. (h) **Where the starting points are.** One row on the first screen (What you want), under the two buttons: "Or start from a situation like yours". A player who passes it has the same 6 taps as before. A player who uses one has 5 taps when all three values are typical, and one more for each empty value. (i) **A starting point also fills setup answers that the situation itself says** (for example Work: Between jobs), and you confirm or change them in setup. Getting married fills Partnered and A partner lives with you, not Married. Right? (j) **The form before a call ends in the game's own sign-up screen.** The account needs an email and a password: the one thing a lead types. I did not make a second way to create an account. (k) **"New lead" needs no database change.** The mark is in the lead's own saved game, and your coach page reads it. The flag goes away when you tap Mark as seen. (l) **The form works only with your invite link.** With `?coach=1` in the link, Share with my coach starts on and the lead shows in your coach view. Without it the page says that no coach sees the game. The link of the form is on your coach page, under "Form before a first call". (m) **Any coach sees a lead.** The game has one kind of coach link today, with no name and no code of one coach in it, so every account with the coach role sees every shared game. With a second coach, a lead would show for both. Fine for now?

663. **RUN 15 (CC-1), part C, Easier (items 12 to 17). What I chose, for your yes or no, and what only you can do.** (a) **The results card shows your FI number in dollars.** You asked for "the headline number". The card of before had ages only and no dollar amount. The card now has: your era with its one line, your FI number (What it could be, rounded to the thousand) with "FI at age N", and the names of your top 3 moves. No other figure. The reveal shows the card small before the two buttons and says what is on it. Is the dollar amount right, or do you want the age only? (b) **The buttons read "Share my card" and "Get this card as an image".** No button says Save, so that nothing looks like a save of the game by hand. (c) **The short menu: which rows are core.** Where am I, Check in, Safe to spend?, What if, Switch person, Glossary, What's new, Take the tour, and all of Settings and account (Theme, Rearrange Home, Save my game, Privacy, Share SLAM, Report a bug or idea, App lock). Is the list right? (d) **What unlocks a row.** A tool shows once the player opens it, from any screen. After the reveal: Retirement odds, Your tax view, Taking money out, Is my money going where I want. After the first week: all of My money and all of People and home. I chose these two unlocks; the program of the web app has unlocks for its calculators, not for the menu of the game. Do you want other unlocks (for example by era)? (e) **Who gets the short menu.** A game that starts on Nov 27 2026 or later. An older game, the demo game, a coach and the admin keep the whole menu, so no tester loses a row they know. Expert on Me has the whole menu. (f) **Assumptions: the growth rate (7%) and the take-out rate (4%) are shown and cannot be changed yet.** In the game the two are constants that the engines read in about 60 places, 47 more places hold the number itself, about 190 lines of words say "7%", "4%" or "25 times", and a check of the web app expects the two constants as they are. A change by the player would give two answers for one number and words that are no longer true. Three assumptions are yours to change today, by a tap, with "Back to the usual value": the age work is optional, the age your money lasts to, and how much Social Security your plan counts (the engines already read these three from your game). Do you want the larger change (one place for the two rates, and every line of words reads it)? It is a run of its own. (g) **Assumptions that are not on the list.** The 8% line for costly debt, the $1,000 of little e and Social Security from age 67 are numbers written in more than one place in the game, with no one name. I did not list them, so that the sheet holds no copy of a number. (h) **The door FIRE opens the starting point Near FI.** That fits a person who is close. For a person who is far from FI there is no starting point: Near FI asks the amount invested (no typical value fits), so it still works, but its two quests (how you take money out, random markets) are early for them. Do you want an eleventh starting point, "Starting toward FI"? I did not invent one. (i) **The door Couple or household opens Getting married**, the only starting point for two people. A household that is not a couple (roommates, a parent) has no starting point of its own. (j) **The row "Or start from a situation like yours" on What you want stays where it is.** The doors on the front page now do that job earlier, so I did not move it. (k) **Done-for-you setup: the coach reads the file inside the client's game.** On the coach page: Open game, then the row "Fill in from a CSV file" on Home. This uses the way a coach already writes to a shared game, so no database change. The file is read in the coach's browser and is sent nowhere; only the monthly totals by kind (Home, Food, Getting around, Bills, Fun, and the pay when there is one line of it) go into the game. Is a button on the coach page itself wanted (upload with no open game)? That needs the reader on the coach page. (l) **A confirmed total is Close, not Exact**, the same as a file the player reads. The player can type their own amount, keep what they had, or undo. (m) **The tour does not start by itself.** Home offers it one time (the row is with the other rows of Home, below the first cards) and the menu has "Take the tour". Do you want it to start by itself the first time a new player lands on Home? (n) **The tour took the place of the three-card tour** (it was in the fire drills file). Nine stops, all on Home: Your four numbers, Show the math, Your next win, Next Steps, My Money, My number (first week only), The menu, Back and forward, Report.

664. **RUN 15 (CC-1), parts D and E, Better and Cheaper (items 18 to 23), and the finish. What I chose, for your yes or no, and what only you can do.** (a) **Where the Confidence and Joy check is asked.** Not in the opening: the taps to your first number stay the same (6). It is one slim line at the top of Home, after your first result, with Answer and Later. Later puts it away for a week. It comes back 30 days after your last check. The menu row is "Confidence and joy check". Is Home the right place, or do you want it on the result screen too? (b) **The two questions, in my words.** "How sure do you feel about your money today?" (1 is not sure at all, 10 is very sure) and "How much joy does your spending bring you these days?" (1 is very little, 10 is a lot). Change the words if you want. (c) **One stored place.** The game already had a confidence rating, 1 to 10, on the Ratios screen. The check uses that same value: a tap in one place shows in the other. The check keeps each tap with its day, so the before and after can show. (d) **A lower number gets no color and no word.** The screen says "Down 3 points since the start." and "Up or down, this is a record of how it felt." (e) **The admin averages show nothing under 5 players**, so one person cannot be picked out. The change (after minus before) also needs 5 players with two checks. Only players with an account are counted (the admin page reads saved games). Is 5 the right floor? (f) **Your year.** It had the change of net worth and the debt paid off. I added the money wins of the year (from Find some money), the change of the two scales inside the year, and the time ledger (what an hour of your work brings home, and how many hours of work your investments did for you at 7%). With fewer than two saved check-ins in the year, net worth and debt say "Not yet" in place of $0. Is this the time ledger line you meant? (g) **The Graduate page is in the menu** ("Graduate: who to call next"), in the long menu (Show everything), not in the short menu of a new player. Do you want it to open by itself at a point of the game, for example at the era of FI? (h) **The Graduate page says "No one pays to be on this page, and SLAM earns nothing if you call them."** This matches the privacy page. If that ever changes, both pages must change. (i) **Pays for itself: the three parts cover different stretches of time.** Fees found are for one year (the fees now, less the same money in a low-cost fund at 0.05%). Bonuses are for one year (the two bonus moves, on their typical values, marked Guess). Interest saved is for the whole payoff (the Debt plan with its extra a month, less the plan without it). The screen says so in Show the math. Do you want one stretch for all three, for example the first 12 months? (j) **Fees count only after one real answer on Investment mix and fees.** While every fee is a guess from typical funds, the part says "Not found yet" and is left out of the sum. (k) **Is your fee one price paid one time?** The calculator and the chart read it so. On the chart the flat fee leaves the portfolio on day one, which is the hardest case for the flat fee. If your fee is yearly or monthly, the math needs one more input. (l) **Where Pays for itself is.** When you open the game of a client from the coach page, Home has a row "Pays for itself" that only you see. An account with the coach role also has it in the menu, under Coach. A player has neither. (m) **The coaching page is public but says "noindex"**, so search engines stay away while it shows placeholders. Take that line out when the page is filled (it is the first comment in `coaching/index.html`). The page also shows an amber note "Draft page" while a value is empty. Do you want the page held back from the public site until you fill it? (n) **The chart with no price.** Until the price is set, the chart shows the same portfolio with no yearly fee next to a 1% yearly fee, and says that the flat-fee line comes once the price is set. (o) **The example values on the chart**: the portfolio today ($50k, $100k, $250k, $500k, $1M; it starts on $250k) and the amount added each year ($0, $6k, $12k, $24k; it starts on $12k). Change them if your clients look different. (p) **The headline "Coaching with Eli" and the line "One offer. One flat price. You keep your accounts, and you keep the game."** are mine. Change them if you want. (q) **Bank and card bonus moves.** The rule for who gets one was in the game: no debt at 8% or more. The card move used it; the bank move did not, though the skill tree said both wait. Now both use it. A player who fits sees both on the first screen of the list "ways to get there sooner", so that list shows up to five rows where it showed three. A player with costly debt sees them under "ways that do not fit you yet, and why". The opening does not ask about debt, so a new player counts as having no costly debt until they list a debt. Is that fine, or do you want one question about card debt before a bonus move shows? (r) **The second eligibility signal, "I pay each card in full each month", is asked on the full card of the move, not in the opening.** A player who has not answered it still sees the card move. Ask it earlier?

665. **RUN FIRE statuses and the life timeline, Part 2: the life timeline. What I chose, for your yes or no.** (a) **One new sheet, not a change of Your life map.** The life map is in the order of the steps; the timeline is in the order of time, by age and year. It has no math of its own: each date is the one its own screen shows. Yes, or should the life map itself become the timeline? (b) **Only one move can be kept into the plan: the age you chose for work to be a choice.** A baby, a career break or a big spend stays a what-if, because the plan has no place for a cost that starts in a future year. To keep one, the plan run (`goalSim`) needs dated costs. Do you want that built? (c) **A milestone of your own moves a date only after the player gave its simulator their own number** (the yearly cost of a child, the months off, the cost of the spend). The simulators have starting amounts (a child: $16,000 a year), but they have no source, so the timeline does not count them. Yes? (d) **Buy a home is a marker with no effect.** The rent or buy simulator compares two paths; it gives no one cost for the plan. Is a marker enough, or do you want a home to become a goal with a price? (e) **Moving the age you chose moves Coast, and not FI.** In the game's engine the FI date comes from spending and saving, not from that age. The band says so ("No other date moves" when nothing does). Is that what you expect to see? (f) **Debt-free comes from the debt plan, the cushion from the plan run.** The two runs pay debt in different ways, so the cushion can show before debt-free. Each card names its run. Should one run own both dates? (g) **Not on the line:** Barista, Chubby and Fat (no number in the game), the ages of kids, a claim age for Social Security other than 67 (the card links to the simulator that compares 62, 67 and 70). (h) **The words** of the kinds, the cards and the band are mine; edit any of them. (i) **The web app has no life timeline.** Do you want it there too?
